1-\frac{\frac{1}{a_{+}}-\frac{1}{a_{-}}}{{2-\frac{1}{a_{-}}}}=\frac{2-\frac{1}{a_{+}}}{2-\frac{1}{a_{-}}}
, with
2-\frac{1}{a_{-}}\rightarrow 2-c_{-}(\nu,t_{\nu})>0
and :

\displaystyle 2-\frac{1}{a_{+}}=4\sqrt{\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\left((z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z_{2})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{z^{+}}}(z^{+}_{\nu}-z_{1})\right)+\mathcal{O}\left((z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z_{2})^{2}+(z^{+}_{\nu}-z_{1})^{2}\right),

as
z_{1}\rightarrow z_{\nu}^{+}
and
z_{2}\rightarrow z^{\diamond}_{\nu}
. So, that plugging this expression in (
4.52
), we get:

I_{p}(z_{1},z_{2})=a^{\prime}_{p}+b^{\prime}_{p}\,(z^{+}_{\nu}-z_{1})+b^{\prime\prime}_{p}(z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z_{2})\\
+c_{p}\left(4\sqrt{\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\left((z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z_{2})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{z^{+}_{\nu}}}(z^{+}_{\nu}-z_{1})\right)\right)^{p+1/2}\\
+\mathcal{O}\left((z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z_{2})^{2}+(z^{+}_{\nu}-z_{1})^{2}\right)

(4.53)

as
z_{1}\to(z^{+})^{-}
and
z_{2}\to(z^{\diamond})^{-}
and where the constants are explicit. Notice that when
p=5/3
, the singular term of the development is of order higher than
2
and will not contribute in the final development.

Finally, we can sum the different contributions to the integral and write:

f^{\diamond}_{\nu}(z_{1},z_{2})=(\nu t_{\nu})^{3/2}(2z_{1}+z_{2}^{2})\\
+\frac{\sqrt{\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}}{\sqrt{a_{+}a_{-}}}\,\left(I_{\varphi}(z_{1},z_{2})+\aleph_{2/3}^{Q^{+}}(\nu)I_{2/3}(z_{1},z_{2})+\aleph_{4/3}^{Q^{+}}(\nu)I_{4/3}(z_{1},z_{2})+\aleph_{5/3}^{Q^{+}}(\nu)I_{5/3}(z_{1},z_{2})\right).

Plugging into this expression the expansions of
I_{\varphi}
,
I_{2/3}
,
I_{4/3}
,
I_{5/3}
, and recalling that
f^{\diamond}
is differentiable at
(z^{+},z^{\diamond})
, we get

\displaystyle f^{\diamond}_{\nu}(z_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle=f^{\diamond}(z^{+}_{\nu},z^{\diamond}_{\nu})-\nabla f^{\diamond}(z^{+}_{\nu},z^{\diamond}_{\nu})\cdot(z^{+}_{\nu}-z_{1},z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z_{2})

\displaystyle\qquad+\aleph^{\diamond}_{2/3}(\nu)\left((z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z_{2})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{z^{+}_{\nu}}}(z^{+}_{\nu}-z_{1})\right)^{2/3+1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\aleph^{\diamond}_{4/3}(\nu)\left((z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z_{2})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{z^{+}_{\nu}}}(z^{+}_{\nu}-z_{1})\right)^{4/3+1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathcal{O}\left((z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z_{2})^{2}+(z^{+}_{\nu}-z_{1})^{2}\right),

with

\aleph^{\diamond}_{p}(\nu)=\frac{c_{p}\left(4\sqrt{\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\right)^{p+1/2}\,\sqrt{\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,\aleph_{p}^{Q^{+}}(\nu)}{\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\frac{1}{y_{-}\left(\nu,t_{\nu}\right)}\right)}}.

Note that we have
\aleph^{\diamond}_{2/3}(\nu_{c})=0
, so we set

\aleph^{\diamond}(\nu)=\begin{cases}\aleph_{2/3}^{\diamond}(\nu)&\text{for $\nu<\nu_{c}$,}\\
\aleph_{4/3}^{\diamond}(\nu_{c})&\text{for $\nu=\nu_{c}$.}\end{cases}

The expansion for
f^{\diamond}_{\nu}
given in the lemma then follows from the fact that
f^{\diamond}(z^{+}_{\nu},z^{\diamond}_{\nu})=z^{\diamond}_{\nu}
and from the criticality of the weight sequence
\mathbf{q}(\nu,t_{\nu})
established in Proposition
4.10
, which ensures the equality

\partial_{z_{2}}f^{\diamond}(z^{+}_{\nu},z^{\diamond}_{\nu})+\sqrt{z^{+}_{\nu}}\partial_{z_{1}}f^{\diamond}(z^{+}_{\nu},z^{\diamond}_{\nu})=1.

We now move to the development for
f^{\bullet}
. It is obtained with the same strategy by replacing
\varphi
by the function

\varphi^{\bullet}(y)=\frac{1-\sqrt{\nu t^{3}_{\nu}}-\frac{1}{y}}{2z_{1}}Q^{+}(t_{\nu},t_{\nu}y)-\frac{1-\sqrt{\nu t^{3}_{\nu}}-\frac{1}{2}}{2z_{1}}\left(\aleph_{2/3}^{Q^{+}}(\nu)\cdot(2-y)^{2/3}+\aleph_{4/3}^{Q^{+}}(\nu)\cdot(2-y)^{4/3}\right).

We can prove as above that the function

I_{\varphi^{\bullet}}(z_{1},z_{2})=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{1}\,\xi^{-1/2}(1-\xi)^{-1/2}\varphi^{\bullet}\left(\frac{1}{a_{-}}+\left(\frac{1}{a_{+}}-\frac{1}{a_{-}}\right)\xi\right)d\xi

is twice differentiable on
[0,z^{+}_{\nu}]\times[0,z^{\diamond}_{\nu}]
. We can then use the identity

f_{\nu}^{\bullet}(z_{1},z_{2})=(\nu t_{\nu})^{3/2}\,2z_{2}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{a_{+}a_{-}}}\,I_{\varphi}(z_{1},z_{2})+\frac{\frac{1}{2}-\sqrt{\nu t^{3}_{\nu}}}{2z_{1}\sqrt{a_{+}a_{-}}}\,\left(\aleph_{2/3}^{Q^{+}}(\nu)I_{2/3}(z_{1},z_{2})+\aleph_{4/3}^{Q^{+}}(\nu)I_{4/3}(z_{1},z_{2})\right)

and the developments of
I_{2/3}
and
I_{4/3}
to establish that

\displaystyle f^{\bullet}_{\nu}(z_{1},z_{2})

\displaystyle=f^{\bullet}(z^{+}_{\nu},z^{\diamond}_{\nu})-\nabla f^{\bullet}(z^{+}_{\nu},z^{\diamond}_{\nu})\cdot(z^{+}_{\nu}-z_{1},z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z_{2})

\displaystyle\qquad+\aleph^{\bullet}_{2/3}(\nu)\left((z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z_{2})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{z^{+}_{\nu}}}(z^{+}_{\nu}-z_{1})\right)^{2/3+1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\aleph^{\bullet}_{4/3}(\nu)\left((z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z_{2})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{z^{+}_{\nu}}}(z^{+}_{\nu}-z_{1})\right)^{4/3+1/2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathcal{O}\left((z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z_{2})^{2}+(z^{+}_{\nu}-z_{1})^{2}\right),

with

\aleph^{\bullet}_{p}(\nu)=\frac{\frac{1}{2}-\sqrt{\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}z^{\diamond}_{\nu}}{2z^{+}_{\nu}}\,\frac{c_{p}\left(4\sqrt{\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\right)^{p+1/2}\,\aleph_{p}^{Q^{+}}(\nu)}{\sqrt{\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\frac{1}{y_{-}\left(\nu,t_{\nu}\right)}\right)}}.

Similarly as before, we set

\aleph^{\bullet}(\nu)=\begin{cases}\aleph_{2/3}^{\bullet}(\nu)&\text{for $\nu<\nu_{c}$,}\\
\aleph_{4/3}^{\bullet}(\nu_{c})&\text{for $\nu=\nu_{c}$.}\end{cases}

The statement for
f^{\bullet}_{\nu}
then follows from the criticality of the weight sequence
\mathbf{q}(\nu,t_{\nu})
that ensures the equality
f^{\bullet}_{\nu}(z^{+}_{\nu},z^{\diamond}_{\nu})=1-1/z^{+}_{\nu}
, and from the two following generic identities for BDG functions:

\forall(z_{1},z_{2}),\quad\partial_{z_{2}}f^{\bullet}(z_{1},z_{2})=\partial_{z_{1}}f^{\diamond}(z_{1},z_{2})\quad\text{and}\quad z_{1}\partial{z_{1}}f^{\bullet}(z_{1},z_{2})+f^{\bullet}(z_{1},z_{2})=\partial_{z_{2}}f^{\diamond}(z_{1},z_{2}).

∎

4.3.3
Singular developments of
z^{+}_{\nu}(g)
and
z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)

For
\nu>0
and
g\in(0,1]
, let us denote by
(z^{+}_{\nu}(g),z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g))
the solution in
(0,z^{+}_{\nu}]\times(0,z^{\diamond}_{\nu}]
of the system (
4.24
) when the weight sequence is
\mathbf{q}(\nu,t_{\nu})
. In other words,
(z^{+}_{\nu}(g),z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g))
is the solution of the system of equations:

\begin{cases}f^{\bullet}_{\nu}\Big(z^{+}_{\nu}(g),z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)\Big)&=1-\dfrac{g}{z^{+}_{\nu}(g))},\\
f^{\diamond}_{\nu}\Big(z^{+}_{\nu}(g),z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)\Big)&=z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g),\end{cases}

(4.54)

where we recall that
f^{\bullet}_{\nu}:=f^{\bullet}_{\mathbf{q}(\nu,t_{\nu})}
and
f^{\diamond}_{\nu}:=f^{\diamond}_{\mathbf{q}(\nu,t_{\nu})}
.

Using the asymptotic expansions of
f^{\bullet}_{\nu}
and
f^{\diamond}_{\nu}
established in Lemma
4.17
, we can obtain the asymptotic behavior of
z^{+}_{\nu}(g)
and
z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)
as
g
approaches
1
:

Lemma 4.19
.

For
\nu\in(0,\nu_{c}]
, we have as
g\to 1^{-}
:

\displaystyle z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)

\displaystyle=z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-\frac{1}{2}\left({\sqrt{z^{+}_{\nu}}}\aleph^{\diamond}(\nu)+z^{+}_{\nu}\aleph^{\bullet}(\nu)\right)^{-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\,\left(1-g\right)^{\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}+o\left(\left(1-g\right)^{\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\right),

(4.55)

\displaystyle z^{+}_{\nu}(g)

\displaystyle=z^{+}_{\nu}-\frac{\sqrt{z^{+}_{\nu}}}{2}\left({\sqrt{z^{+}_{\nu}}}\aleph^{\diamond}(\nu)+z^{+}_{\nu}\aleph^{\bullet}(\nu)\right)^{-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\,\left(1-g\right)^{\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}+o\left(\left(1-g\right)^{\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\right),

(4.56)

where we recall that

a(\nu)=\begin{cases}5/3&\text{for $\nu<\nu_{c}$,}\\
7/3&\text{for $\nu=\nu_{c}$.}\end{cases}\quad\text{ and hence }\quad\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}=\begin{cases}6/7&\text{for $\nu<\nu_{c}$,}\\
6/11&\text{for $\nu=\nu_{c}$.}\end{cases}

(4.57)

Remark 4.20
.

As for Lemma
4.17
, we could establish a similar development for
z^{+}_{\nu}(g)
and
z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)
for
\nu>\nu_{c}
, with a singular term of order
(1-g)^{1/2}
. Since it could also be obtained directly with the regular criticality of the weight sequence in this regime (and is not needed for the rest of the article), we do not include it here.

Proof of Lemma
4.19
.

As explained at the end of Section
4.1.3
, the functions
g\mapsto z^{+}_{\nu}(g)
and
g\mapsto z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)
are positive and increasing in
g
, and we have
\Big(z^{+}_{\nu}(g),z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)\Big)\to(z^{+}_{\nu},z^{\diamond}_{\nu})
as
g\to 1^{-}
.

Fix
\nu\in(0,\nu_{c}]
. We start from the expansion for
f^{\diamond}_{\nu}
established in Lemma
4.17
, where we take
z_{1}=z_{\nu}^{+}(g)
and
z_{2}=z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)
. Since, by definition
f^{\diamond}_{\nu}\Big(z^{+}_{\nu}(g),z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)\Big)=z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)
(see (
4.54
)), this gives:

z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)=\partial_{z_{1}}f^{\diamond}_{\nu}(z^{+}_{\nu},z^{\diamond}_{\nu})\cdot\Big(z^{+}_{\nu}-z^{+}_{\nu}(g)\Big)+\left(1-\sqrt{z^{+}_{\nu}}\partial_{z_{1}}f^{\diamond}_{\nu}(z^{+}_{\nu},z^{\diamond}_{\nu})\right)\cdot\Big(z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)\Big)\\
-\aleph^{\diamond}(\nu)\left(\Big(z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)\Big)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{z^{+}_{\nu}}}\Big(z^{+}_{\nu}-z^{+}_{\nu}(g)\Big)\right)^{a(\nu)-1/2}\\
+\mathcal{O}\left(\Big(z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)\Big)^{2}+\Big(z^{+}_{\nu}-z^{+}_{\nu}(g)\Big)^{2}\right).

As a consequence, we have:

z^{+}_{\nu}-z^{+}_{\nu}(g)={\sqrt{z^{+}_{\nu}}}\,\Big(z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)\Big)+\frac{\aleph^{\diamond}(\nu)}{\partial_{z_{1}}f^{\diamond}_{\nu}(z^{+}_{\nu},z^{\diamond}_{\nu})}\left(2\Big(z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)\Big)\right)^{a(\nu)-1/2}+o\left(\Big(z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)\Big)^{a(\nu)-1/2}\right).

(4.58)

Now, we take
z_{1}=z_{\nu}^{+}(g)
and
z_{2}=z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)
in the development for
f^{\bullet}_{\nu}
obtained in Lemma
4.17
. Combined with the fact that
f^{\bullet}_{\nu}\Big(z^{+}_{\nu}(g),z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)\Big)=1-\frac{g}{z^{+}_{\nu}(g)}
, we get:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{z^{+}_{\nu}}-\frac{g}{z^{+}_{\nu}(g)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{z^{+}_{\nu}}\left(\frac{1}{z^{+}_{\nu}}-\sqrt{z^{+}_{\nu}}\partial_{z_{1}}f^{\diamond}_{\nu}(z^{+}_{\nu},z^{\diamond}_{\nu})\right)\left(z^{+}_{\nu}-z^{+}_{\nu}(g)\right)-\partial_{z_{1}}f^{\diamond}_{\nu}(z^{+}_{\nu},z^{\diamond}_{\nu})\left(z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\aleph^{\bullet}(\nu)\left(\Big(z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)\Big)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{z^{+}_{\nu}}}\Big(z^{+}_{\nu}-z^{+}_{\nu}(g)\Big)\right)^{a(\nu)-1/2}+\mathcal{O}\left(\Big(z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)\Big)^{2}+\Big(z^{+}_{\nu}-z^{+}_{\nu}(g)\Big)^{2}\right),

Then, replacing
z^{+}_{\nu}-z^{+}_{\nu}(g)
by its expansion given in (
4.58
) yields:

\frac{1}{z^{+}_{\nu}}-\frac{g}{z^{+}_{\nu}(g)}=-\frac{1}{(z^{+}_{\nu})^{3/2}}\,\Big(z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)\Big)\\
-\frac{1}{z^{+}_{\nu}}\left(\frac{1}{z^{+}_{\nu}}-\sqrt{z^{+}_{\nu}}\partial_{z_{1}}f^{\diamond}_{\nu}(z^{+}_{\nu},z^{\diamond}_{\nu})\right)\frac{\aleph^{\diamond}(\nu)}{\partial_{z_{1}}f^{\diamond}_{\nu}(z^{+}_{\nu},z^{\diamond}_{\nu})}\left(2\Big(z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)\Big)\right)^{a(\nu)-1/2}\\
+\aleph^{\bullet}(\nu)\left(2\Big(z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)\Big)\right)^{a(\nu)-1/2}+o\left(\Big(z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)\Big)^{a(\nu)-1/2}\right).

(4.59)

On the other hand, we may also write:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{z^{+}_{\nu}}-\frac{g}{z^{+}_{\nu}(g)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1-g}{z^{+}_{\nu}}+\frac{g}{z^{+}_{\nu}}\left(1-\frac{z^{+}\nu}{z^{+}_{\nu}(g)}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1-g}{z^{+}_{\nu}}-\frac{g}{(z^{+}_{\nu})^{2}}\left(z^{+}_{\nu}-z^{+}_{\nu}(g)\right)+\mathcal{O}\left(\Big(z^{+}_{\nu}-z^{+}_{\nu}(g)\Big)^{2}\right)

Using again the expansion of
z^{+}_{\nu}-z^{+}_{\nu}(g)
obtained in (
4.58
), we get:

\frac{1}{z^{+}_{\nu}}-\frac{g}{z^{+}_{\nu}(g)}=\frac{1-g}{z^{+}_{\nu}}-\frac{g}{(z^{+}_{\nu})^{3/2}}\Big(z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)\Big)\\
-\frac{g}{(z^{+}_{\nu})^{2}}\frac{\aleph^{\diamond}(\nu)}{\partial_{z_{1}}f^{\diamond}_{\nu}(z^{+}_{\nu},z^{\diamond}_{\nu})}\left(2\Big(z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)\Big)\right)^{a(\nu)-1/2}+o\left(\Big(z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)\Big)^{a(\nu)-1/2}\right).

(4.60)

Identifying the right-hand sides of both (
4.59
) and (
4.60
) gives the following equality:

\frac{1-g}{z^{+}_{\nu}}+\frac{1-g}{(z^{+}_{\nu})^{3/2}}\Big(z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)\Big)+\frac{1-g}{(z^{+}_{\nu})^{2}}\frac{\aleph^{\diamond}(\nu)}{\partial_{z_{1}}f^{\diamond}_{\nu}(z^{+}_{\nu},z^{\diamond}_{\nu})}\left(2\Big(z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)\Big)\right)^{a(\nu)-1/2}\\
=\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{z^{+}_{\nu}}}\aleph^{\diamond}(\nu)+\aleph^{\bullet}(\nu)\right)\left(2\Big(z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)\Big)\right)^{a(\nu)-1/2}+o\left(\Big(z^{\diamond}_{\nu}-z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)\Big)^{a(\nu)-1/2}\right),

from which the singular development for
z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)
follows.

The expansion for
z^{+}_{\nu}(g)
can then be obtained by plugging the expansion for
z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)
in (
4.58
).
∎

5
root spin cluster in finite random triangulations

5.1
root spin cluster in Ising Boltzmann triangulations (proof of Theorem
1.3
)

5.1.1
Perimeter distribution and regular criticality of
\mathbf{q}(\nu,t_{\nu})

We start by proving the statements of Theorem
1.3
about the perimeter of the root spin cluster.
The proof relies on the fact that under
\mathbb{P}^{\nu}
, the root spin cluster is a Boltzmann random map with weight sequence
\mathbf{q}(\nu,t_{\nu})
, as stated in Proposition
4.9
. We have:

\mathbb{P}^{\nu}\left(|\partial\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu})|=l\right)=\frac{w_{\mathbf{q}(\nu,t_{\nu})}(\mathcal{M}_{l})}{\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}(\nu,t_{\nu})},

where we recall that
\mathcal{M}_{l}
is the set of planar maps with a root face of degree
l
.
By definition of the disk generating series
W^{(l)}_{\mathbf{q}(\nu,t_{\nu})}
, given in (
4.3
), the Boltzmann weight of clusters with perimeter
l\geqslant 1
is given by

w_{\mathbf{q}(\nu,t_{\nu})}(\mathcal{M}_{l})=W^{(l)}_{\mathbf{q}(\nu,t_{\nu})}\cdot q_{l}(\nu,t_{\nu}).

Therefore, we have

\mathbb{P}^{\nu}\left(|\partial\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu})|=l\right)=\frac{W^{(l)}_{\mathbf{q}(\nu,t_{\nu})}\cdot q_{l}(\nu,t_{\nu})}{\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}(\nu,t_{\nu})}=\frac{(t_{\nu}\nu)^{-l/2}Q_{l}^{+}(\nu,t_{\nu})\cdot q_{l}(\nu,t_{\nu})}{\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}(\nu,t_{\nu})},

where the last equality comes from (
4.31
). Define
\mathrm{C}^{\mathrm{p}}_{\mathrm{bol}}(\nu)
by:

\mathrm{C}^{\mathrm{p}}_{\mathrm{bol}}(\nu):=\frac{(y_{\nu}-1)^{a(\nu)}\Big(\aleph^{Q^{+}}(\nu)\Big)^{2}}{\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}(\nu,t_{\nu})\left(\Gamma\Big(1-a(\nu)\Big)\right)^{2}}.

(5.1)

Then, the asymptotics given in Corollary
3.11
and Proposition
4.13
yield:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\nu}\left(\partial\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu})=l\right)

\displaystyle\underset{l\to\infty}{\sim}\mathrm{C}^{\mathrm{p}}_{\mathrm{bol}}(\nu)(t_{\nu}\nu)^{-l/2}\times\left({t_{\nu}}\right)^{-l}\left({y(\nu,t_{\nu})}\right)^{-l}l^{-a(\nu)}\times\left(\frac{y(\nu,t_{\nu})}{y(\nu,t_{\nu})-1}\,t_{\nu}^{3/2}\nu^{1/2}\right)^{l}l^{-a(\nu)}

\displaystyle\underset{l\to\infty}{\sim}\mathrm{C}^{\mathrm{p}}_{\mathrm{bol}}(\nu)\,\left(\frac{1}{y(\nu,t_{\nu})-1}\,\right)^{l}l^{-2a(\nu)},

(5.2)

The statements about the perimeter tail distribution from Theorem
1.3
follow since
y(\nu,t_{\nu})=2
for
\nu\leqslant\nu_{c}
and
y(\nu,t_{\nu})>2
for
\nu>\nu_{c}
.

A direct consequence of this result is the following classification of
\mathbf{q}(\nu,t_{\nu})
according to Definition
4.3
:

Proposition 5.1
.

The weight sequence
\mathbf{q}(\nu,t_{\nu})
is:

•

non-regular critical for
\nu\leqslant\nu_{c}
.

•

regular critical for
\nu>\nu_{c}
.

5.1.2
Volume distribution

We now move to the volume tail distribution and start with the (much easier) case
\nu>\nu_{c}
where the cluster is a regular critical Boltzmann map, and finally we establish the volume tail behavior in the case
\nu\leqslant\nu_{c}
where the root spin cluster is a non generic critical Boltzmann map.

Volume tail when
\nu>\nu_{c}
.

In this range of values for
\nu
, Proposition
5.1
states that
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu})
is a regular critical Boltzmann map and the statement follows by Proposition
4.4
.

Volume tail when
\nu\leqslant\nu_{c}
.

In this regime, the map
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu})
is non-regular critical and we have to compute the generating function of its volume.

For
g\leqslant 1
, recall the definition of
z^{+}(g)
and
z^{\diamond}(g)
given in (
4.54
).
Equation (
4.21
) states that:

\mathbb{E}^{\nu}\left[|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}))|\,g^{|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}))|}\right]=g\,\frac{2(z^{+}(g)-g)+(z^{\diamond}(g))^{2}}{\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}(\nu,t_{\nu})}.

(5.3)

The expansions of
z^{+}(g)
and
z^{\diamond}(g)
obtained in Lemma
4.19
imply that
\mathbb{E}^{\nu}\left[|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}))|\,g^{|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}))|}\right]
has a singular expansion as
g\to 1^{-}
of the form:

\mathbb{E}^{\nu}\left[|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}))|\,g^{|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}))|}\right]=\mathbb{E}^{\nu}\left[|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}))|\right]-\mathrm{C}(\nu)(1-g)^{\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}+o\left((1-g)^{\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\right),

(5.4)

with
\mathrm{C}(\nu)>0
.

Unfortunately, this expansion is not enough to extract the asymptotic behavior of the coefficients in
g
of
\mathbb{E}^{\nu}\left[|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu})|\,g^{|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu})|}\right]
since it could have complex singularities of modulus
1
other than
1
. To work around this problem, we rely on a related series in
g
with decreasing coefficients and Tauberian theorems, as we now explain.

It follows directly from (
5.4
) that:

\frac{\mathbb{E}^{\nu}\left[|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}))|\right]-\mathbb{E}^{\nu}\left[|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}))|\,g^{|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}))|}\right]}{1-g}\underset{g\to 1^{-}}{\sim}\mathrm{C}(\nu)(1-g)^{\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}-1},

where the exponent
\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}-1
is negative for every
\nu\leqslant\nu_{c}
, since
a(\nu)=5/3
for
\nu<\nu_{c}
and
a(\nu_{c})=7/3
.

On the other hand, denoting
p_{n}=\mathbb{P}^{\nu}\left(|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}))|=n\right)
, we also have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}^{\nu}\left[|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}))|\right]-\mathbb{E}^{\nu}\left[|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}))|\,g^{|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}))|}\right]}{1-g}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\geqslant 0}\left(\mathbb{E}^{\nu}\left[|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}))|\right]-\sum_{k=0}^{n}kp_{k}\right)\,g^{n},

\displaystyle=\sum_{n\geqslant 0}\sigma_{n}\,g^{n},

with
\sigma_{n}:=\sum_{k>n}kp_{k}
positive and decreasing with
n
. From there, a classical Tauberian theorem (see e.g. Theorem VI.13 of
[
36
]
and the following discussion) implies that:

\sigma_{n}\underset{n\to\infty}{\sim}\mathrm{C}(\nu)\frac{n^{-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}\right)}.

To complete the proof of Theorem
1.3
, we want to relate this last asymptotic behavior to the behavior of
s_{n}=\sum_{k>n}p_{k}
. Since

\sum_{k\geqslant n}s_{k}=\sum_{l>n}p_{l}(l-n)=\sigma_{n}-ns_{n},

we can again use Tauberian methods to establish the asymptotic behavior of
s_{n}
. Indeed, for
\varepsilon>0
, we have:

ns_{n}-n(1+\varepsilon)s_{n(1+\varepsilon)}+\sum_{k=n}^{n(1+\varepsilon)-1}s_{n}=\sigma_{n}-\sigma_{n(1+\varepsilon)},

which implies

ns_{n}-n(1+\varepsilon)s_{n(1+\varepsilon)}+\sum_{k=n}^{n(1+\varepsilon)-1}s_{n}\\
=\frac{\mathrm{C}(\nu)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}\right)}\,n^{-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\,\left(1-(1+\varepsilon)^{-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\right)+o\left(n^{-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\right).

(5.5)

Using the fact that
s_{n}
is decreasing, we get

n(1+\varepsilon)s_{n}\geqslant n(1+\varepsilon)s_{n(1+\varepsilon)}+\frac{\mathrm{C}(\nu)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}\right)}\,n^{-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\,\left(1-(1+\varepsilon)^{-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\right)+o\left(n^{-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\right).

Multiplying by
n^{\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}
and taking the
\varliminf
as
n\to\infty
on both sides we get:

\displaystyle(1+\varepsilon)\varliminf\left(n^{1+\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}s_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant\quad

\displaystyle(1+\varepsilon)^{-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\varliminf\left(\Big(n(1+\varepsilon)\Big)^{1+\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}s_{n(1+\varepsilon)}\right)\ +\frac{\mathrm{C}(\nu)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}\right)}\left(1-(1+\varepsilon)^{-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\right),

\displaystyle\geqslant\quad

\displaystyle(1+\varepsilon)^{-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\varliminf\left(n^{1+\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}s_{n}\right)+\frac{\mathrm{C}(\nu)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}\right)}\left(1-(1+\varepsilon)^{-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\right).

Letting
\varepsilon\to 0
in the last display yields

\varliminf\left(n^{1+\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}s_{n}\right)\geqslant\frac{\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}{1+\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\frac{\mathrm{C}(\nu)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}\right)}=\frac{1}{a(\nu)+1/2}\frac{\mathrm{C}(\nu)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}\right)}.

(5.6)

We can establish an inequality for the
\varlimsup
in a similar fashion. Indeed, starting from (
5.5
) and using the fact that
s_{n}
decreases with
n
, we get

ns_{n}-ns_{n(1+\varepsilon)}\leqslant\frac{\mathrm{C}(\nu)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}\right)}\,n^{-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\,\left(1-(1+\varepsilon)^{-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\right)+o\left(n^{-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\right).

Multiplying by
n^{\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}
and taking the
\varlimsup
as
n\to\infty
on both sides we get

\displaystyle\varlimsup\left(n^{1+\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}s_{n}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\quad

\displaystyle\varlimsup\left(n^{1+\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}s_{n(1+\varepsilon)}\right)+\frac{\mathrm{C}(\nu)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}\right)}\,n^{-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\,\left(1-(1+\varepsilon)^{-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\right),

\displaystyle\leqslant\quad

\displaystyle(1+\varepsilon)^{-1-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\varlimsup\left(n^{1+\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}s_{n}\right)+\frac{\mathrm{C}(\nu)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}\right)}\,n^{-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\,\left(1-(1+\varepsilon)^{-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\right).

Letting
\varepsilon\to 0
in the last display finally gives

\varlimsup\left(n^{1+\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}s_{n}\right)\leqslant\frac{1}{a(\nu)+1/2}\frac{\mathrm{C}(\nu)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}\right)}.

(5.7)

By combining inequalities (
5.6
) and (
5.7
), we get:

s_{n}=\mathbb{P}^{\nu}\left(|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}))|>n\right)\underset{n\to\infty}{\sim}\frac{1}{a(\nu)+1/2}\frac{\mathrm{C}(\nu)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}\right)}\,n^{-\frac{a(\nu)+1/2}{a(\nu)-1/2}}.

(5.8)

This finishes the proof of Theorem
1.3
since the exponent in the previous expansion is
-13/7
in the high temperature case when
a(\nu)=5/3
, and
-17/11
in the critical temperature case when
a(\nu)=7/3
.

5.2
root spin cluster in large random Ising triangulations (proof of Theorem
1.4
)

Recall from (
1.5
) that
\mathbf{T}_{n}^{\nu}
denotes a random triangulation with law
\mathbb{P}^{\nu}
conditioned to have
3n
edges, meaning:

\forall(\mathfrak{t},\sigma)\in\mathcal{T}^{\oplus}\,,\quad\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\nu}\left(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}_{n}=(\mathfrak{t},\sigma)\right)=\frac{\nu^{m(\mathfrak{t},\sigma)}}{[t^{3n}]\,\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}(\nu,t)}\mathbf{1}_{\{|\mathfrak{t}|=3n\}}.

It follows from Proposition
4.8
, that the law of
\mathfrak{C}\left(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}_{n}\right)
is given by:

\forall\mathfrak{m}\in\mathcal{M}\,,\quad\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\nu}\left(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{n}^{\nu})=\mathfrak{m}\right)=\frac{[t^{3n}]\prod_{f\in F(\mathfrak{m})}q_{\mathrm{deg}(f)}(\nu,t)}{[t^{3n}]\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}(\nu,t)},

and therefore

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{n}^{\nu}\left[|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{n}^{\nu}))|\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{[t^{3n}]\sum_{\mathfrak{m}\in\mathcal{M}}|V(\mathfrak{m})|\,\prod_{f\in F(\mathfrak{m})}q_{\mathrm{deg}(f)}(\nu,t)}{[t^{3n}]\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}(\nu,t)},

\displaystyle=\frac{[t^{3n}]w_{\mathbf{q}(\nu,t)}\left(\mathcal{M}^{\bullet}\right)}{[t^{3n}]\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}(\nu,t)}.

Equations (
4.5
) and (
4.8
) then give

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{n}^{\nu}\left[|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{n}^{\nu}))|\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{[t^{3n}]\left(W_{\mathbf{q}(\nu,t),\bullet}^{(2)}-1\right)}{[t^{3n}]\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}(\nu,t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{[t^{3n}]\left(\frac{3}{8}c_{+}^{2}(\nu,t)+\frac{3}{8}c_{-}^{2}(\nu,t)+\frac{1}{4}c_{+}(\nu,t)c_{-}(\nu,t)\right)}{[t^{3n}]\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}(\nu,t)}.

We can then use the link between the quantities
c_{\pm}(\nu,t)
and the singularities
y_{\pm}(\nu,t)
of
Q^{+}(\nu,t,ty)
given in (
4.33
) to get

\mathbb{E}_{n}^{\nu}\left[|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{n}^{\nu}))|\right]=\frac{[t^{3n}]\frac{1}{\nu t^{3}}\left(\frac{3}{8}\frac{1}{y_{+}(\nu,t)^{2}}+\frac{3}{8}\frac{1}{y_{-}(\nu,t)^{2}}+\frac{1}{4}\frac{1}{y_{+}(\nu,t)y_{-}(\nu,t)}\right)}{[t^{3n}]\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}(\nu,t)}.

(5.9)

Proposition
3.15
ensures that the numerator of (
5.9
) has asymptotics of the form
\mathrm{Cst}\,t_{\nu}^{-3n}\,n^{-\gamma_{+}(\nu)}
, with
\gamma_{+}(\nu)=7/4
for
\nu<\nu_{c}
and
\gamma_{+}(\nu)=3/2
for
\nu>\nu_{c}
. Moreover, the asymptotics for
[t^{3n}]\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}(\nu,t)
are of the form
\mathrm{Cst}\,(t_{\nu})^{-3n}\,n^{-\gamma(\nu)}
, with
\gamma(\nu)=5/2
for
\nu\neq\nu_{c}
and
\gamma(\nu)=7/3
for
\nu=\nu_{c}
. Therefore we have

\mathbb{E}_{n}^{\nu}\left[|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{n}^{\nu}))|\right]\underset{n\to\infty}{\sim}\mathrm{Cst}\,n^{\gamma(\nu)-\gamma_{+}(\nu)},

finishing the proof of Theorem
1.4
.

6
root spin cluster in the IIPT

6.1
Cluster volume generating function and percolation probability

We start by identifying the law of the root spin cluster in the IIPT on the event where it is finite.

Proposition 6.1
.

For any non atomic finite rooted map
\mathfrak{m}
and every
\nu>0
one has

\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\nu}\left(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{\infty}^{\nu})=\mathfrak{m}\right)=\left(\prod_{f\in F(\mathfrak{m})}q_{\mathrm{deg}(f)}(\nu,t_{\nu})\right)\cdot\sum_{f\in F(\mathfrak{m})}\frac{(\nu t_{\nu}^{3})^{\mathrm{deg}(f)/2}\,\delta_{\mathrm{deg}(f)}(\nu)}{q_{\mathrm{deg}(f)}(\nu,t_{\nu})},

(6.1)

where the numbers
\left(\delta_{k}(\nu)\right)_{k\geqslant 1}
are defined in Proposition
4.14
.

Proof.

We start from (
4.29
), which can be written as:

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\nu}\left(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{n}^{\nu})=\mathfrak{m}\right)=\frac{[t^{3n}]\left((\nu t^{3})^{|\mathfrak{m}|}\cdot\prod_{f\in F(\mathfrak{m})}(\nu t^{3})^{-\mathrm{deg}(f)/2}q_{\mathrm{deg}(f)}(\nu,t)\right)}{[t^{3n}]\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}(\nu,t)}.

Proposition
4.14
then states that, for every
k
, the asymptotic behavior of
[t^{3n}]\left((\nu t^{3})^{-k/2}q_{k}(\nu,t)\right)
are of the form
\mathrm{Cst}_{k}\cdot t_{\nu}^{-3n}n^{-\gamma(\nu)}
with
\gamma(\nu)>2
, which is also the asymptotic behavior of
[t^{3n}]\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}(\nu,t)
. We also proved in the same lemma that the ratio of these asymptotics converge towards
\delta_{k}(\nu)
for every
k
. From there, classical calculations give the announced formula.
∎

Our starting point to prove Theorem
1.1
and Theorem
1.2
will be the next statement, which establishes integral formulae for the probability that the root spin cluster is finite and for the generating function of the volume of the root spin cluster.

Proposition 6.2
.

Recall the definition of the function
\Delta
given in Proposition
4.14
and set
c_{\pm}^{\nu}:=c_{\pm}(\nu,t_{\nu}
). For every
\nu>0
one has

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\nu}

\displaystyle(|\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{\infty}^{\nu}))|<\infty)=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{c_{-}^{\nu}}^{c_{+}^{\nu}}\frac{dz}{z}\Delta\left(\nu,\sqrt{\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,z\right)\left(z+\frac{c_{+}^{\nu}+c_{-}^{\nu}}{2}\right)\sqrt{(c_{+}^{\nu}-z)(z-c_{-}^{\nu})}.

(6.2)

In addition, for
g\in(0,1)
one has

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\infty}^{\nu}

\displaystyle\left[g^{|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{\infty}^{\nu}))|}\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{g}{2\pi}\int_{c_{-}^{\nu}(g)}^{c_{+}^{\nu}(g)}\frac{dz}{z}\Delta\left(\nu,\sqrt{g\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,z\right)\left(z+\frac{c_{+}^{\nu}(g)+c_{-}^{\nu}(g)}{2}\right)\sqrt{(c_{+}^{\nu}(g)-z)(z-c_{-}^{\nu}(g))},

(6.3)

where

c_{\pm}^{\nu}(g):=c_{\pm}(\mathbf{q}_{g}(\nu,t_{\nu})).

(6.4)

Proof.

By Proposition
6.1
, for any
\nu>0
and any non atomic map
\mathfrak{m}
one has

\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\nu}\left(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}_{\infty})=\mathfrak{m}\right)=\sum_{f\in F(\mathfrak{m})}(\nu t_{\nu}^{3})^{\mathrm{deg}(f)/2}\,\delta_{\mathrm{deg}(f)}\prod_{f^{\prime}\in F(\mathfrak{m}),f^{\prime}\neq f}q_{\mathrm{deg}(f^{\prime})}(\nu,t_{\nu}).

We want to sum this probability over finite rooted non atomic maps
\mathfrak{m}\in\mathcal{M}
. This will be easier to manage if we sum maps with a marked face (the face denoted
f^{\prime}
in the above expression).

Let
\mathfrak{m}_{\square}:=(\mathfrak{m},f_{\square})
be a non-atomic rooted map
\mathfrak{m}
, with an additional marked face
f_{\square}
, such that
f_{\square}
is different from the root face
f_{r}
of
\mathfrak{m}
. The Boltzmann weight
w_{\square}(\mathfrak{m}_{\square})
of
\mathfrak{m}_{\square}
is defined as:

w_{\square}(\mathfrak{m}_{\square})=\prod_{f\in F(\mathfrak{m})\setminus\{f_{r},f_{\square}\}}q_{\mathrm{deg}(f)}(\nu,t_{\nu}).

For
l_{1},l_{2}\geqslant 1
, let us denote by
\mathcal{M}_{\square}^{(l_{1},l_{2})}
the set all maps with root face degree
l_{1}
and a marked face of degree
l_{2}
, and set

W_{\square}^{(l_{1},l_{2})}=\sum_{\mathfrak{m}_{\square}\in\mathcal{M}_{\square}^{(l_{1},l_{2})}}w_{\square}(\mathfrak{m}_{\square}).

By rooting the marked face so that it lies on the left side of this new root, we see that

W_{\square}^{(l_{1},l_{2})}=\frac{1}{l_{2}}W^{(l_{1},l_{2})}_{2},

where we recall that
W^{(l_{1},l_{2})}_{2}
is the generating function of
\mathbf{q}(\nu,t_{\nu})
-Boltzmann maps of the cylinder with perimeters
l_{1}
and
l_{2}
defined in (
4.25
).

We then have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\nu}(|\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{\infty}^{\nu})|<\infty)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathfrak{m}\in\mathcal{M}}\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\nu}(\mathfrak{C}=\mathfrak{m}),

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathfrak{m}\in\mathcal{M}_{f}^{(2)}}\,\sum_{f\in F(\mathfrak{m})\setminus\{f_{r}\}}\,(\nu t_{\nu}^{3})^{\mathrm{deg}(f)/2}\,\delta_{\mathrm{deg}(f)}\prod_{f^{\prime}\in F(\mathfrak{m}),f^{\prime}\neq f,f_{r}}q_{\mathrm{deg}(f^{\prime})}(\nu,t_{\nu}),

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathfrak{m}\in\mathcal{M}_{f,\square}^{(2)}}\,(\nu t_{\nu}^{3})^{\mathrm{deg}(f_{\square})/2}\,\delta_{\mathrm{deg}(f_{\square})}\,w_{\square}(\mathfrak{m}_{\square}),

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geqslant 1}(\nu t_{\nu}^{3})^{k/2}\,\,\delta_{k}\cdot W_{\square}^{(2,k)},

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\geqslant 1}(\nu t_{\nu}^{3})^{k/2}\,\,\delta_{k}\cdot\frac{1}{k}W^{(2,k)}_{2}.

The last display is the Hadamard product of two generating series evaluated at
z=1
. Similarly to what was done in the proof of Proposition
4.16
(see also Appendix
B.2
), we can write:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\nu}(|\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{\infty}^{\nu})|<\infty)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2i\pi}\oint_{\gamma}\frac{dz}{z}\Delta\left(\nu,\sqrt{\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,z\right)\cdot\left(\sum_{k\geqslant 1}\frac{1}{k}W_{2}^{(2,k)}z^{-k}\right),

(6.5)

where the integration contour
\gamma
is inside a domain where both functions
\Delta(\nu,\sqrt{\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,z)
and
\sum_{k\geqslant 1}\frac{1}{k}W_{2}^{(2,k)}z^{-k}
are analytic.

To check that such a contour exists and to be able to evaluate the integral,
we will compute
\sum_{k\geqslant 1}\frac{1}{k}W_{2}^{(2,k)}z^{-k}
below in (
6.6
). The expression we obtain shows that this function is analytic on
\mathbb{C}\setminus[c_{-}^{\nu},c_{+}^{\nu}]
. On the other hand, from Proposition
4.14
, we have that:

\Delta\left(\nu,\sqrt{\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,z\right)=\frac{1}{\beth^{\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}}(\nu)}\,\frac{\sqrt{\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,z}{1-\sqrt{\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,z}\beth^{Q^{+}}\left(\nu,\frac{1}{1-\sqrt{\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,z}\right).

The parametric expression of
\beth^{Q^{+}}\left(\nu,y\right)
in terms of
V(\nu,U_{\nu},y)
ensures that it is analytic on
\mathbb{C}\setminus\left((-\infty,y_{-}(\nu,t_{\nu}]\cup[y_{\nu},+\infty)\right)
. Therefore, the function
\Delta\left(\nu,\sqrt{\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,z\right)
is analytic on
\mathbb{C}\setminus[z_{\nu},\infty)
, where
z_{\nu}
is computed in (
4.37
) and satisfies

z_{\nu}=c_{+}^{\nu}\cdot(y_{\nu}-1)\geqslant c_{+}^{\nu}

since
y_{\nu}\geqslant 2
.

As in the proof of Proposition
4.16
, when
z_{\nu}=c_{+}^{\nu}
(which corresponds to
\nu\leqslant\nu_{c}
), we cannot directly find a suitable contour
\gamma
. However, we can fortunately first compute

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2i\pi}\oint_{\gamma}\frac{dz}{z}\Delta\left(\nu,\sqrt{\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,w\,z\right)\cdot\left(\sum_{k\geqslant 1}\frac{1}{k}W_{2}^{(2,k)}z^{-k}\right),

for
w<1
where a contour exists and take the limit as
w\to 1^{-}
.

To evaluate
\sum_{k\geqslant 1}\frac{1}{k}W_{2}^{(2,k)}z^{-k}
, we first apply Proposition
4.6
(with weight sequence
\mathbf{q}(\nu,t_{\nu})
) to get:

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geqslant 1}W_{2}^{(2,k)}z^{-k-1}

\displaystyle=[z_{1}^{-3}]W_{2}(z_{1},z)

\displaystyle=-z+W_{\bullet}(z)\,\left(z^{2}-\frac{c_{+}^{\nu}+c_{-}^{\nu}}{2}z+\frac{1}{4}c_{+}^{\nu}c_{-}^{\nu}-\frac{1}{8}\left((c_{+}^{\nu})^{2}+(c_{-}^{\nu})^{2}\right)\right).

This can be rewritten as:

\frac{d}{dz}\left(\sum_{k\geqslant 1}\frac{1}{k}W_{2}^{(2,k)}z^{-k}\right)=z-W_{\bullet}(z)\,\left(z^{2}-\frac{c_{+}^{\nu}+c_{-}^{\nu}}{2}z+\frac{1}{4}c_{+}^{\nu}c_{-}^{\nu}-\frac{1}{8}\left((c_{+}^{\nu})^{2}+(c_{-}^{\nu})^{2}\right)\right).

The antiderivative has a simple expression:

\sum_{k\geqslant 1}\frac{1}{k}W_{2}^{(2,k)}z^{-k}=\frac{z^{2}}{2}-\frac{z+\frac{c_{+}^{\nu}+c_{-}^{\nu}}{2}}{2W_{\bullet}(z)}=\frac{z^{2}}{2}-\frac{z+\frac{c_{+}^{\nu}+c_{-}^{\nu}}{2}}{2}\sqrt{(z-c_{+}^{\nu})(z-c_{-}^{\nu})},

(6.6)

which gives:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\nu}(|\mathfrak{C}|<\infty)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2i\pi}\oint_{\gamma}\frac{dz}{z}\Delta\left(\nu,\sqrt{\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,z\right)\cdot\left(\frac{z^{2}}{2}-\frac{z+\frac{c_{+}^{\nu}+c_{-}^{\nu}}{2}}{2}\sqrt{(z-c_{+}^{\nu})(z-c_{-}^{\nu})}\right),

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{4i\pi}\oint_{\gamma}\frac{dz}{z}\Delta\left(\nu,\sqrt{\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,z\right)\cdot\left(z+\frac{c_{+}^{\nu}+c_{-}^{\nu}}{2}\right)\sqrt{(z-c_{+}^{\nu})(z-c_{-}^{\nu})}.

By deforming the contour
\gamma
so that it surrounds the cut
[c_{-}^{\nu},c_{+}^{\nu}]
where the root is not analytic, we finally obtain:

\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\nu}(|\mathfrak{C}|<\infty)=-\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{c_{-}^{\nu}}^{c_{+}^{\nu}}\frac{dz}{z}\Delta\left(\nu,\sqrt{\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,z\right)\cdot\left(z+\frac{c_{+}^{\nu}+c_{-}^{\nu}}{2}\right)\sqrt{(c_{+}^{\nu}-z)(z-c_{-}^{\nu})}.

To establish the second formula of the proposition, we also start from (
6.1
). Euler’s formula implies that, for every
\mathfrak{m}\in\mathcal{M}
and
g\in(0,1)
:

g^{|V(\mathfrak{m})|}\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\nu}\Big(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{\infty}^{\nu})=\mathfrak{m}\Big)=g\,\sum_{f\in F(\mathfrak{m})}g^{\mathrm{deg}(f)/2}\,(\nu t_{\nu}^{3})^{\mathrm{deg}(f)/2}\delta_{\mathrm{deg}(f)}\\
\cdot\left(\prod_{f^{\prime}\in F(\mathfrak{m}),f^{\prime}\neq f}g^{(\mathrm{deg}(f^{\prime})-2)/2}q_{\mathrm{deg}(f^{\prime})}(\nu,t_{\nu})\right).

(6.7)

We can then perform the exact same sequence of computations as above, but with the weight sequence
\mathbf{q}_{g}(\nu,t_{\nu})
(introduced in (
4.20
) of Section
4.1.3
) instead of the weight sequence
\mathbf{q}(\nu,t_{\nu})
.
We obtain in this way:

\mathbb{E}_{\infty}^{\nu}\left[g^{|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{\infty}^{\nu}))|}\right]\\
=-\frac{g}{2\pi}\int_{c_{-}^{\nu}(g)}^{c_{+}^{\nu}(g)}\frac{dz}{z}\Delta\left(\nu,\sqrt{g\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,z\right)\cdot\left(z+\frac{c_{+}^{\nu}(g)+c_{-}^{\nu}(g)}{2}\right)\sqrt{(c_{+}^{\nu}(g)-z)(z-c_{-}^{\nu}(g))},

which finishes the proof.
∎

6.2
Percolation probability and critical exponent (proof of Theorem
1.1
)

The root spin cluster is finite almost surely when
\nu\leqslant\nu_{c}
.

We prove the first statement of the theorem by computing explicitly the probability that the root spin cluster in the IIPT is finite for
\nu\leqslant\nu_{c}
. To do so, we rely on the integral formula obtained in Proposition
6.2
.

Recall that
c_{\pm}^{\nu}=1/(\sqrt{\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}y_{\pm}^{\nu})
.
The change of variables
z=(1-1/y)(\nu t_{\nu}^{3})^{-1/2}
in (
6.2
) gives

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\nu}(|\mathfrak{C}|<\infty)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\int_{\frac{y_{-}^{\nu}}{y_{-}^{\nu}-1}}^{\frac{y_{+}^{\nu}}{y_{+}^{\nu}-1}}\frac{dy}{y(y-1)}\Delta\left(\nu,\frac{y-1}{y}\right)\left(\frac{y-1}{y}+\frac{\frac{1}{y_{+}^{\nu}}+\frac{1}{y_{-}^{\nu}}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\sqrt{\left(\frac{1}{y_{+}^{\nu}}-\frac{y-1}{y}\right)\left(\frac{y-1}{y}-\frac{1}{y_{-}^{\nu}}\right)},

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}\,\beth^{\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}}(\nu)}\int_{\frac{y_{-}^{\nu}}{y_{-}^{\nu}-1}}^{\frac{y_{+}^{\nu}}{y_{+}^{\nu}-1}}\frac{dy}{y}\,\beth^{Q^{+}}\left(\nu,y\right)\left(\frac{y-1}{y}+\frac{\frac{1}{y_{+}^{\nu}}+\frac{1}{y_{-}^{\nu}}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\sqrt{\left(\frac{1}{y_{+}^{\nu}}-\frac{y-1}{y}\right)\left(\frac{y-1}{y}-\frac{1}{y_{-}^{\nu}}\right)},

where we used the expression of
\Delta
in terms of
\beth^{Q^{+}}
given in Proposition
4.14
.

To compute this integral, we perform the change of variable
y=\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)
, which allows in particular to use the explicit formula for
\beth^{Q^{+}}\!\!(\nu,\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V))
. We start by computing the new bounds of the integral, which amounts to solving for
V
the two equations:

\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)=\frac{y_{\pm}^{\nu}}{y_{\pm}^{\nu}-1}\Leftrightarrow\frac{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)-1}{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)}=\frac{1}{y_{\pm}^{\nu}}.

When
\nu\leqslant\nu_{c}
, it is easily done since

\frac{1}{y_{+}^{\nu}}-\frac{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)-1}{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)}=\frac{2U_{\nu}-3}{24(1-U_{\nu})}\,\frac{(1-V)^{3}}{V(V+1)},

and

\frac{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)-1}{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)}-\frac{1}{y_{-}^{\nu}}=\frac{2U_{\nu}-3}{24(1-U_{\nu})}\,\frac{(V+2+\sqrt{3})^{2}(V-7+4\sqrt{3})}{V(V+1)}.

Since
y_{+}^{\nu}
and
y_{-}^{\nu}
correspond respectively to
V
equal to
1
and to
-2+\sqrt{3}
in the parametrization
y=\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)
, the only legitimate solution for the second equation is
V=7-4\sqrt{3}
. We then obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\nu}(|\mathfrak{C}|<\infty)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}\,\beth^{\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}}(\nu)}\int_{7-4\sqrt{3}}^{1}dV\,\frac{\partial_{V}\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)}{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)}\,\beth^{Q^{+}}\left(\nu,\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left(\frac{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)-1}{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)}+\frac{\frac{1}{y_{+}^{\nu}}+\frac{1}{y_{-}^{\nu}}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\sqrt{\left(\frac{1}{y_{+}^{\nu}}-\frac{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)-1}{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)}\right)\left(\frac{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)-1}{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)}-\frac{1}{y_{-}^{\nu}}\right)},

(6.8)

with as a bonus a fully factorized rational fraction under the square root. There are a lot of cancellations in the other terms and the integral is easy to compute with Maple. It turns out that the value of this probability is
1
and we refer to the Maple file
[
1
]
for the explicit computation.

The root spin cluster is infinite with positive probability when
\nu>\nu_{c}
.

The explicit formula (
6.11
) that we obtain for the probability that the root spin cluster is infinite when
\nu>\nu_{c}
is awful and we prefer to give a simpler argument that does not depend on this formula.
Indeed, in this temperature regime, a general argument already found in
[
10
, proof of Theorem 1.2]
can be readily applied to our setting as follows.

We established in Proposition
5.1
that, for
\nu>\nu_{c}
, the root spin cluster under
\mathbb{P}^{\nu}
is a regular critical Boltzmann map. Proposition
4.12
then states that for any
\nu>\nu_{c}
, there exists
c
such that

\mathbb{P}^{\nu}\left(|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}))|\geqslant n\right)\underset{n\to\infty}{\sim}c\,n^{-3/2}.

However, for
\nu\neq\nu_{c}
, Corollary
2.6
gives

\mathbb{P}^{\nu}\left(|V(\mathbf{T}^{\nu})|\geqslant n\right)\underset{n\to\infty}{\sim}C\,n^{-3/2},

for some
C>0
depending on
\nu
. In addition, the event
\{|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}))|\geqslant n\}
is the same as the event
\{|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu})|\geqslant n,|V(\mathbf{T}^{\nu})|\geqslant n\}
. Hence, we have for
n
large enough

\mathbb{P}^{\nu}\left(|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}))|\geqslant n\,\Big|\,|V(\mathbf{T}^{\nu})|\geqslant n\right)\geqslant\frac{c}{2C}>0.

Then, for every
n
large enough and any
N\leqslant n
, we have
\mathbb{P}^{\nu}\left(|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}))|\geqslant N\,\Big|\,|V(\mathbf{T}^{\nu})|\geqslant n\right)\geqslant\frac{c}{2C}
. Since the event
\{|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}))|\geqslant N\}
is a local event, taking the limit as
n\to\infty
yields
\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\nu}\left(|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}_{\infty}))|\geqslant N\right)\geqslant\frac{c}{2C}
. This last statement being true for any
N
, the root spin cluster is therefore infinite with positive probability when
\nu>\nu_{c}
.

Explicit formula and critical exponent
\beta
.

We start from the formula for the probability that the root spin cluster is finite obtained in Proposition
6.2
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\nu}(|\mathfrak{C}|<\infty)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}\,\beth^{\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}}(\nu)}\int_{\frac{y_{-}^{\nu}}{y_{-}^{\nu}-1}}^{\frac{y_{+}^{\nu}}{y_{+}^{\nu}-1}}\frac{dy}{y}\,\beth^{Q^{+}}\left(\nu,y\right)\left(\frac{y-1}{y}+\frac{\frac{1}{y_{+}^{\nu}}+\frac{1}{y_{-}^{\nu}}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\sqrt{\left(\frac{1}{y_{+}^{\nu}}-\frac{y-1}{y}\right)\left(\frac{y-1}{y}-\frac{1}{y_{-}^{\nu}}\right)}.

To compute this integral and then do an asymptotic expansion as
\nu\to\nu_{c}^{+}
, we will use the change of variable
y=\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)
with
\nu
and
U_{\nu}
parametrized by
K_{\nu}
according to (
2.9
) and (
2.10
).
This relation also gives that as
K_{\nu}\to K_{\nu_{c}}^{+}
we have

K_{\nu}-K_{\nu_{c}}=\frac{56+14\sqrt{7}}{81}(\nu-\nu_{c})+o\left(\nu-\nu_{c}\right),

(6.9)

so we can do an asymptotic expansion in
K
.
The main difficulty will come from the fact that several roots and poles of the rational functions in
V
that are in the integral converge to
1
at the same time and we have to be careful to analyze each contribution separately.

We proceed similarly as for
\nu\leqslant\nu_{c}
and start by computing the bounds in
V
for the integral after the change of variables. This means solving for
V
the equation

\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)=\frac{y_{\pm}^{\nu}}{y_{\pm}^{\nu}-1},

where we recall that
y_{\pm}^{\nu}
are the positive and negative singularities of
Q_{+}(\nu,t_{\nu},t_{\nu}y)
. Via the rational parametrization, they correspond respectively to
V_{+}(K_{\nu})\in(0,1)
and
V_{-}(K_{\nu})<0
, for which we have the explicit expressions from (
3.15
):

\displaystyle V_{+}(K_{\nu})

\displaystyle=\frac{2(K_{\nu}+1)^{2}-\sqrt{(K_{\nu}^{2}+4K_{\nu}+5)(3K_{\nu}^{2}+4K_{\nu}-1)}}{3-K_{\nu}^{2}},

\displaystyle V_{-}(K_{\nu})

\displaystyle=\frac{-(K_{\nu}^{2}+4K_{\nu}+5)+2\sqrt{2}\sqrt{(K_{\nu}+2)(K_{\nu}+1)^{2}}}{3-K_{\nu}^{2}}.

We thus have to solve for
V
the two equations of degree three

\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)=\frac{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V_{+}(K_{\nu}))}{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V_{+}(K_{\nu}))-1}\quad\text{and}\quad\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)=\frac{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V_{-}(K_{\nu}))}{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V_{-}(K_{\nu}))-1}.

Fortunately we have the relation

\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},1/V)=\frac{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)}{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)-1},

and therefore we have to solve

\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)=\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},1/V_{+}(K_{\nu}))\quad\text{and}\quad\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)=\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},1/V_{-}(K_{\nu})).

We obtain readily that
1/V_{+}(K_{\nu})
and
1/V_{-}(K_{\nu})
are respective solutions of the two equations. But, we can moreover check that they are in fact double roots of each equation. Furthermore, the third solution of each equation is given respectively by
V_{+}(K_{\nu})^{2}
and
V_{-}(K_{\nu})^{2}
. See the Maple file
[
1
]
for the detailed calculations.

Since
0<V_{+}(K_{\nu})<1
, we have
0<V_{+}(K_{\nu})^{2}<V_{+}(K_{\nu})
and
1/V_{+}(K_{\nu})>V_{+}(K_{\nu})
. Similarly, we also have
1/V_{-}(K_{\nu})<V_{-}(K_{\nu})
and
0<-V_{-}(K_{\nu})<V_{+}(K_{\nu})
. The bounds for the integral after the change of variable
y=\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)
are then
V_{-}(K_{\nu})^{2}
and
V_{+}(K_{\nu})^{2}
. We thus have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\nu}(|\mathfrak{C}|<\infty)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\pi\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}\,\beth^{\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}}(\nu)}\int_{V_{-}(K_{\nu})^{2}}^{V_{+}(K_{\nu})^{2}}dV\,\frac{\partial_{V}\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)}{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)}{\beth^{Q^{+}}\left(\nu,\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)\right)}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\left(\frac{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)-1}{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)}+\frac{\frac{1}{y_{+}^{\nu}}+\frac{1}{y_{-}^{\nu}}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\sqrt{\left(\frac{1}{y_{+}^{\nu}}-\frac{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)-1}{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)}\right)\left(\frac{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)-1}{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)}-\frac{1}{y_{-}^{\nu}}\right)}.

Since we identify all the roots of the factors of the square root, it is fortunately already factorized, and only the constant coefficient needs to be identified. We can hence write:

\displaystyle\sqrt{\left(\frac{1}{y_{+}^{\nu}}-\frac{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)-1}{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)}\right)\left(\frac{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)-1}{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)}-\frac{1}{y_{-}^{\nu}}\right)}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\left(\frac{1}{y_{+}^{\nu}}-\frac{1}{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},1/V)}\right)\left(\frac{1}{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},1/V)}-\frac{1}{y_{-}^{\nu}}\right)}

\displaystyle=f_{1}(K_{\nu})\frac{\sqrt{\left({V_{+}(K_{\nu})^{2}}-V\right)\left(V-V_{-}(K_{\nu})^{2}\right)}}{V(V+1)}\left(\frac{1}{V_{+}(K_{\nu})}-V\right)\left(V-\frac{1}{V_{-}(K_{\nu})}\right),

for any
V\in[V_{-}(K_{\nu})^{2},V_{+}(K_{\nu})^{2}]
, where
f_{1}(K_{\nu})
is an explicit rational function of
K_{\nu}
.

We turn our attention to the other factors in the integral. A second factor is also easily factorized. Indeed we have:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\nu t_{\nu}^{3}\,\beth^{\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}}(\nu)}

\displaystyle\frac{\partial_{V}\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)}{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)}{\beth^{Q^{+}}\!\!\left(\nu,\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)\right)}=f_{2}(K_{\nu})\,\frac{\left(V^{2}-V\frac{3-K_{\nu}^{2}}{(K_{\nu}+1)^{2}}+1\right)}{\left(V-V_{+}(K_{\nu})\right)^{2}\left(V-\frac{1}{V_{+}(K_{\nu})}\right)^{2}},

where
f_{2}(K_{\nu})
is another explicit rational function of
K_{\nu}
.
Note that the two complex roots roots of the numerator in the last display as well as the four roots
1/V_{\pm}(K_{\nu})
and
V_{\pm}^{i}(K_{\nu})
all converge to
1
as
K_{\nu}\to K_{\nu_{c}}^{+}
.

The last factor in the integral is of the form:

\displaystyle\frac{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)-1}{\hat{Y}(\nu,U_{\nu},V)}+\frac{\frac{1}{y_{+}^{\nu}}+\frac{1}{y_{-}^{\nu}}}{2}=\frac{\mathrm{Pol}_{3}(K_{\nu},V)}{V(V+1)},

where
\mathrm{Pol}_{3}(K_{\nu},V)
is a polynomial of degree
3
in
V
with explicit coefficients in
K_{\nu}
whose roots will not matter for our analysis.

We now have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\nu}(|\mathfrak{C}|<\infty)

\displaystyle=\frac{f(K_{\nu})}{2\pi}\int_{V_{-}(K_{\nu})^{2}}^{V_{+}(K_{\nu})^{2}}dV\,\frac{\mathrm{Pol}_{3}(K_{\nu},V)\cdot\left(V-\frac{1}{V_{-}(K_{\nu})}\right)}{V^{2}(V+1)^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\frac{V^{2}-\frac{3-K_{\nu}^{2}}{(K_{\nu}+1)^{2}}V+1}{\left(V-V_{+}(K_{\nu})\right)^{2}\left(\frac{1}{V_{+}(K_{\nu})}-V\right)}\sqrt{\left({V_{+}(K_{\nu})^{2}}-V\right)\left(V-V_{-}(K_{\nu})^{2}\right)},

(6.10)

with
f(K_{\nu})
an explicit rational function of
K_{\nu}
.

This last integral can be computed explicitly but it requires a bit of work. Indeed Maple does not seem able to compute it directly and needs our help. First, after doing a partial fraction decomposition, the integral we want to compute can be decomposed into a sum of integrals of the following form:

\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{V_{-}(K_{\nu})^{2}}^{V_{+}(K_{\nu})^{2}}dV\,\sqrt{\left({V_{+}(K_{\nu})^{2}}-V\right)\left(V-V_{-}(K_{\nu})^{2}\right)}\,\frac{\mathrm{Pol}(V)}{(V-\mathrm{pole})^{j}},

where
\mathrm{Pol}
is a polynomial of degree
6
in
V
,
\mathrm{pole}\in\{0,-1,V_{+}(K_{\nu}),1/V_{+}(K_{\nu})\}
, and
j\in\{1,2\}
. Then, after doing the change of variable
V=V_{-}(K_{\nu})^{2}+\left(V_{+}(K_{\nu})^{2}-V_{-}(K_{\nu})^{2}\right)\xi
, we are left with integrals of the form

\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{1}d\xi\,\sqrt{\xi(1-\xi)}\,\frac{\xi^{i}}{(1-z(\mathrm{pole})\,\xi)^{j}},

where
\mathrm{pole}\in\{0,-1,V_{+}(K_{\nu}),1/V_{+}(K_{\nu})\}
,
j\in\{1,2\}
,
i\in\{0,\ldots,6\}
, and where

z(\mathrm{pole})=\frac{V_{+}(K_{\nu})^{2}-V_{-}(K_{\nu})^{2}}{\mathrm{pole}-V_{-}(K_{\nu})^{2}}<1.

Each of these integrals has an explicit rational expression in terms of
z(\mathrm{pole})
and
\sqrt{1-z(\mathrm{pole})}
. Summing every contribution gives an explicit formula in terms of
K_{\nu}
for the probability
\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\nu}(|\mathfrak{C}|<\infty)
. See the Maple file
[
1
]
for the detailed computations.

The first expression we obtain with Maple is terrible. However, with some work, we were able to simplify it significantly into the following expression valid for
\nu\geqslant\nu_{c}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\nu}(|\mathfrak{C}|<\infty)={}\frac{1}{2\left(K_{\nu}^{2}+4K_{\nu}+1\right)\left(7K_{\nu}^{2}+20K_{\nu}+15\right)}\,\left({\frac{3K_{\nu}^{2}+4K_{\nu}-1}{K_{\nu}^{2}+4K_{\nu}+5}}\right)^{1/4}\left(\frac{\left(3K_{\nu}^{2}+8K_{\nu}+7\right)\left(K_{\nu}+1\right)}{\left(3-K_{\nu}^{2}\right)^{3}}\right)^{1/2}

(6.11)

\displaystyle\Bigg[(K_{\nu}+1)\bigg(\sqrt{2}\left(2+K_{\nu}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}\left(145K_{\nu}^{8}+1160K_{\nu}^{7}+4612K_{\nu}^{6}+11832K_{\nu}^{5}+23430K_{\nu}^{4}+39000K_{\nu}^{3}+46916K_{\nu}^{2}+31912K_{\nu}+8753\right)

\displaystyle+162\left(K_{\nu}^{8}+8K_{\nu}^{7}+\frac{76}{3}K_{\nu}^{6}+\frac{2872}{81}K_{\nu}^{5}+\frac{518}{81}K_{\nu}^{4}-\frac{3752}{81}K_{\nu}^{3}-\frac{5308}{81}K_{\nu}^{2}-\frac{3416}{81}K_{\nu}-\frac{1039}{81}\right)\,\left(K_{\nu}^{3}+3K_{\nu}^{2}+9K_{\nu}+11\right)\bigg)

\displaystyle-\left(3K_{\nu}^{2}+4K_{\nu}-1\right)^{3/2}\,\sqrt{K_{\nu}^{2}+4K_{\nu}+5}\,\bigg(32\sqrt{2}\,\left(2+K_{\nu}\right)^{\frac{3}{2}}\left(K_{\nu}^{3}+3K_{\nu}^{2}+9K_{\nu}+11\right)\left(K_{\nu}+1\right)^{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{307K_{\nu}^{8}}{8}+{307K_{\nu}^{7}}+\frac{2179K_{\nu}^{6}}{2}+{2133K_{\nu}^{5}}+\frac{10185K_{\nu}^{4}}{4}+{2337K_{\nu}^{3}}+\frac{4211K_{\nu}^{2}}{2}+{1343K_{\nu}}+\frac{2579}{8}\bigg)\Bigg]^{1/2}

We tried to simplify this expression further but were not able to. If the reader has some ideas on how to proceed, we would welcome any suggestions. In particular, the last big term should have a factor
(3-K^{2})^{3}
, which would cancel out the same factor in the third term.

The asymptotic behavior of the probability when
\nu\to\nu_{c}^{+}
is driven by the factor with exponent
1/4
. Indeed, all the other factors do not cancel at
K_{\nu_{c}}
, while
K_{\nu_{c}}
is a root of the polynomial
3K_{\nu}^{2}+4K_{\nu}-1
. Doing an asymptotic expansion as
K_{\nu}\to K_{\nu_{c}}^{+}
of our expression and plugging the expansion (
6.9
) of
K
in terms of
\nu
finally gives the expansion of Theorem
1.1
.

6.3
Perimeter and volume critical exponents (proof of Theorem
1.2
)

6.3.1
Perimeter distribution

We start from (
4.29
) that we reproduce here:

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\nu}\left(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{n}^{\nu})=\mathfrak{m}\right)=\frac{[t^{3n}]\prod_{f\in F(\mathfrak{m})}q_{\mathrm{deg}(f)}(\nu,t)}{[t^{3n}]\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}(\nu,t)}.

Summing over maps in
\mathcal{M}_{k}
gives

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\nu}\left(|\partial\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{n}^{\nu})|=k\right)=\frac{[t^{3n}]\left(q_{k}(\nu,t)\cdot W_{\mathbf{q}(\nu,t)}^{(k)}\right)}{[t^{3n}]\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}(\nu,t)}.

Proposition
4.10
then gives

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\nu}\left(|\partial\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{n}^{\nu})|=k\right)=\frac{[t^{3n}]\left(\sqrt{\nu t^{3}}^{-k}\,q_{k}(\nu,t)\cdot t^{k}Q^{+}_{k}(\nu,t)\right)}{[t^{3n}]\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}(\nu,t)}.

(6.12)

From this expression, Proposition
4.14
and Proposition
3.12
give

\lim_{n\to\infty}\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\nu}\left(|\partial\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{n}^{\nu})|=k\right)=\delta_{k}(\nu)\,t_{\nu}^{k}Q_{k}^{+}(\nu,t_{\nu})+q_{k}(\nu,t_{\nu})\,\sqrt{\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}^{-k}\,\frac{[y^{k}]\beth^{Q^{+}}(\nu,y)}{\beth^{\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}}(\nu)}.

(6.13)

Case
\nu\leqslant\nu_{c}
.

In this range of values for
\nu
, we know from Theorem
1.1
that the root spin cluster is almost surely finite. Therefore, in this regime, the perimeter of the root spin cluster of the IIPT is a finite random variable whose law is given by the above limits.

Proposition
3.14
and Proposition
4.13
ensure that

\displaystyle[y^{k}]\beth^{Q^{+}}(\nu,y)

\displaystyle\underset{k\to\infty}{\sim}\frac{\aleph_{4/3}^{\beth^{Q^{+}}}(\nu)}{\Gamma(4/3)}2^{-k}\,k^{1/3},

\displaystyle q_{k}(\nu,t_{\nu})\,\sqrt{\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}^{-k}

\displaystyle\underset{k\to\infty}{\sim}\frac{\aleph^{Q^{+}}(\nu)}{\Gamma(1-a(\nu))}\,2^{k}\,k^{-a(\nu)}.

Corollary
3.11
and Corollary
4.15
give similarly

\displaystyle t_{\nu}^{k}\,Q^{+}_{k}(\nu,t_{\nu})

\displaystyle\underset{k\to\infty}{\sim}\frac{\aleph^{Q^{+}}(\nu)}{\Gamma\Big(1-a(\nu)\Big)}\,2^{-k}\,k^{-a(\nu)},

\displaystyle\delta_{k}(\nu)

\displaystyle\underset{k\to\infty}{\sim}\frac{1}{\beth^{\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}}(\nu)}\frac{\aleph_{4/3}^{\beth^{Q^{+}}}(\nu)}{2^{4/3}\,\Gamma(4/3)}\,2^{k}\,k^{1/3}.

From there, it is easy to see that we have:

\mathbb{P}_{\infty}\left(|\partial\mathfrak{C}|=k\right)\underset{k\to\infty}{\sim}\frac{\aleph^{Q^{+}}(\nu)\,\aleph_{4/3}^{\beth^{Q^{+}}}(\nu)}{\beth^{\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}}(\nu)\,\Gamma(4/3)\,\Gamma\Big(1-a(\nu)\Big)}\,\left(1+2^{-4/3}\right)\,k^{-(a(\nu)-1/3)}

(6.14)

where
a(\nu)=5/3
if
\nu<\nu_{c}
and
a(\nu_{c})=7/3
. The statements on the tail distribution of the perimeter of the root spin cluster for
\nu\leqslant\nu_{c}
follow.

Case
\nu>\nu_{c}
.

When
\nu>\nu_{c}
, the root spin cluster is infinite with positive probability and we have to prove first that its perimeter is finite almost surely. One possibility would be to prove that the probabilities (
6.13
) sum to 1, and then establish their asymptotic behavior, which will be of order
(y_{\nu}-1)^{-k}
with polynomial corrections in
k
. A less tedious solution that suffices for our purpose is to establish exponential bounds for the probabilities
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\nu}\left(|\partial\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{n}^{\nu})|=k\right)
which hold uniformly in
n
.

We start again from (
6.12
). Fix
\nu>\nu_{c}
and
y\in(2,y_{+}^{\nu})
. For every
n\geqslant 0
, we have

\sum_{k\geqslant 1}[t^{3n}]\left(t^{k}\,Q^{+}_{k}(\nu,t)\right)y^{k}=[t^{3n}]\left(\sum_{k\geqslant 1}t^{k}Q^{+}_{k}(\nu,t)y^{k}\right)=[t^{3n}]Q^{+}\left(\nu,t,ty\right),

and therefore, for every
n\geqslant 1
and
k\geqslant 1
, we have

[t^{3n}]t^{k}\,Q^{+}_{k}(\nu,t)\leqslant y^{-k}\,[t^{3n}]Q^{+}\left(\nu,t,ty\right).

To get a similar bound for the second factor of the numerator of (
6.12
), we first observe that
q_{k}(\nu,t)\sqrt{\nu t^{3}}^{-k}
is the formal power series in
t^{3}
for
k\neq 3
and is a formal Laurent series, whose expansion starts with
t^{-3}
for
k=3
. Hence, for the same range of
y
as above and
n\geqslant-1
:

\displaystyle\sum_{k\geqslant 1}[t^{3n}]

\displaystyle\left(q_{k}(\nu,t)\sqrt{\nu t^{3}}^{-k}\right)\cdot(1-1/y)^{k}

\displaystyle=[t^{3n}]\left(\sum_{k\geqslant 1}q_{k}(\nu,t)\sqrt{\nu t^{3}}^{-k}\cdot(1-1/y)^{k}\right),

\displaystyle=[t^{3n}]\left((\nu t)^{3/2}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\nu t^{3}}}(1-1/y)\right)^{3}+\frac{(1-1/y)}{1-(1-1/y)}Q^{+}\left(\nu,t,\frac{t}{1-(1-1/y)}\right)\right),

\displaystyle=(1-1/y)^{3}\mathbf{1}_{\{n=-1\}}+[t^{3n}]\left(\frac{y-1}{y}Q^{+}\left(\nu,t,ty\right)\right).

Therefore, for every
n\geqslant 0
and
k\geqslant 1
, we also have

[t^{3n}]\left(q_{k}(\nu,t)\sqrt{\nu t^{3}}^{-k}\right)\leqslant(1-1/y)^{-k}[t^{3n}]\left(\frac{y-1}{y}Q^{+}\left(\nu,t,ty\right)\right).

Combining the bounds we obtained, we get that for every
n\geqslant 1
and every
k\geqslant 1
, if
y\in(2,y_{+}^{\nu})
, then

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\nu}\left(|\partial C(\mathbf{T}_{n}^{\nu})|=k\right)\leqslant\frac{(y-1)^{-k}[t^{3n}]\left(\frac{y-1}{y}\left(Q^{+}\left(\nu,t,ty\right)\right)^{2}\right)}{[t^{3n}]\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}(\nu,t)}

Since the coefficients in
t
of
Q^{+}\left(\nu,t,ty\right)
are increasing in
y
, the asymptotics of Lemma
3.12
show that for every
\nu>\nu_{c}
and
y\in(2,y_{+}(\nu,t_{\nu}))
, there exists a constant
C(\nu)
such that, for every
k\geqslant 1
and every
n\geqslant 1
, one has

\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\nu}\left(|\partial C(\mathbf{T}_{n}^{\nu})|=k\right)\leqslant C(\nu)\,(y-1)^{-k}.

Since
y>2
, this proves that the perimeters of the root spin cluster under
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\nu}
for
n\geqslant 1
form a tight sequence of random variables. Since we know that each probability
\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\nu}\left(|\partial\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{n}^{\nu})|=k\right)
converges, the random variables
|\partial\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{n}^{\nu})|
converge in law towards a limiting random variable with exponential tail. This finishes the proof of the statement of Theorem
1.2
on the distribution of the perimeter of the root spin cluster in the low temperature regime.

6.3.2
Volume distribution

In this whole section, we only consider
\nu\leqslant\nu_{c}
. We start with the second formula of Proposition
6.2
: for
\nu>0
and
g\in(0,1)
we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\infty}^{\nu}

\displaystyle\left[g^{|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{\infty}^{\nu}))|}\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{g}{2\pi}\int_{c_{-}^{\nu}(g)}^{c_{+}^{\nu}(g)}\frac{dz}{z}\Delta\left(\nu,\sqrt{g\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,z\right)\cdot\left(z+\frac{c_{+}^{\nu}(g)+c_{-}^{\nu}(g)}{2}\right)\sqrt{(c_{+}^{\nu}(g)-z)(z-c_{-}^{\nu}(g))},

\displaystyle=-\frac{g}{2\pi\,\beth^{\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}}(\nu)}\int_{c_{-}^{\nu}(g)}^{c_{+}^{\nu}(g)}dz\,\frac{\sqrt{g\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}}{1-\sqrt{g\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,z}\beth^{Q^{+}}\left(\nu,\frac{1}{1-\sqrt{g\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,z}\right)\cdot\left(z+\frac{c_{+}^{\nu}(g)+c_{-}^{\nu}(g)}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\sqrt{(c_{+}^{\nu}(g)-z)(z-c_{-}^{\nu}(g))}.

We want an asymptotic expansion as
g\to 1^{-}
of the previous quantity. It relies on two things: first we established the asymptotic expansion of
z^{+}(g)
and
z^{\diamond}(g)
at
g=1
in Lemma
4.19
; second we established the analytic properties and singular behavior at
y=2
of the function
y\mapsto\beth^{Q^{+}}(\nu,y)
in Proposition
3.14
. Combining the two will lead to the desired expansion.

The change of variable
z(\nu,g,\xi)=c_{-}^{\nu}(g)+\left(c_{+}^{\nu}(g)-c_{-}^{\nu}(g)\right)\xi
in the last display gives the following identity:

\mathbb{E}_{\infty}^{\nu}\left[g^{|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{\infty}^{\nu}))|}\right]=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{1}d\xi\,\sqrt{\xi(1-\xi)}\,\phi_{1}(\nu,g,\xi)\,\phi_{2}(\nu,g,\xi),

with

\displaystyle\phi_{1}(\nu,g,\xi)

\displaystyle=-\frac{g}{2}\,\frac{\sqrt{g\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}}{1-\sqrt{g\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,z(\nu,g,\xi)}\,\left(z(\nu,g,\xi)+\frac{c_{+}^{\nu}(g)+c_{-}^{\nu}(g)}{2}\right)\,\left(c_{+}^{\nu}(g)-c_{-}^{\nu}(g)\right)^{2},

\displaystyle\phi_{2}(\nu,g,\xi)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\beth^{\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}}(\nu)}\,\beth^{Q^{+}}\left(\nu,\frac{1}{1-\sqrt{g\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,z(\nu,g,\xi)}\right).

The asymptotic expansions for
z^{+}_{\nu}(g)
and
z^{\diamond}_{\nu}(g)
obtained in (
4.55
) and (
4.56
) can be translated into the following expansions for
c_{+}^{\nu}(g)
and
c_{-}^{\nu}(g)
via (
4.22
):

\displaystyle c_{+}^{\nu}(g)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2\sqrt{\nu t^{3}_{\nu}}}-\left({\sqrt{z^{+}_{\nu}}}\aleph^{\diamond}(\nu)+z^{+}_{\nu}\aleph^{\bullet}(\nu)\right)^{-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\,\left(1-g\right)^{\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}+o\left(\left(1-g\right)^{\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\right),

\displaystyle c_{-}^{\nu}(g)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\nu t^{3}_{\nu}}\,y_{-}(\nu,t_{\nu})}+o\left(\left(1-g\right)^{\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\right),

where every constant is explicit. This in turn gives the following expansion in
g
for
\phi_{1}

\phi_{1}(\nu,g,\xi)=\phi_{1}(\nu,1,\xi)+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(1-g\right)^{\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\right),

where the error term is uniform in
\xi\in[0,1]
. Therefore we have

\mathbb{E}_{\infty}^{\nu}\left[g^{|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{\infty}^{\nu}))|}\right]=\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{1}d\xi\,\sqrt{\xi(1-\xi)}\,\phi_{1}(\nu,1,\xi)\,\phi_{2}(\nu,g,\xi)+\mathcal{O}\left(\left(1-g\right)^{\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\right).

(6.15)

Indeed, we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathbb{E}_{\infty}^{\nu}\left[g^{|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{\infty}^{\nu}))|}\right]-\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{1}d\xi\,\sqrt{\xi(1-\xi)}\,\phi_{1}(\nu,1,\xi)\,\phi_{2}(\nu,g,\xi)\right|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\leqslant\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{1}d\xi\,\sqrt{\xi(1-\xi)}\,|\phi_{1}(\nu,1,\xi)-\phi_{1}(\nu,g,\xi)|\,\phi_{2}(\nu,g,\xi),

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\leqslant\mathrm{C}(\nu)\,\left(1-g\right)^{\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{1}d\xi\,\sqrt{\xi(1-\xi)}\,\,\phi_{2}(\nu,g,\xi),

with
\mathrm{C}(\nu)
a positive constant. We will see that the integral in the last display is bounded with
g
, which will establish (
6.15
).

To study the dependency in
g
of the integral in (
6.15
), we will proceed in a similar fashion than in the proof of Lemma
4.17
by subtracting for
\beth^{Q^{+}}
its singular expansion obtained in Proposition
3.14
. Recall from this proposition, that
\beth^{Q^{+}}(\nu,y)
is analytic on
\{y\,\Big|\,|y|\leqslant 2\text{ and }y\neq 2\}
, and has a unique dominant singularity at
y_{\nu}=2
. Hence,
\phi_{2}(\nu,g,\xi)
is analytic for
(g,\xi)\in(0,1]\times[0,1]\backslash\{(1,1)\}
.

Hence, if we set

\varphi(\nu,g,\xi)=\phi_{1}(\nu,1,\xi)\phi_{2}(\nu,g,\xi)-\frac{\phi_{1}(\nu,1,1)}{\beth^{\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}}(\nu)}\,\sum_{i\in\left\{\frac{4}{3},\frac{2}{3},\frac{1}{3},0,-\frac{1}{3},-\frac{2}{3}\right\}}\frac{\aleph_{i}^{\beth^{Q^{+}}}(\nu)}{\left(2-\frac{1}{1-\sqrt{g\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,z(\nu,g,\xi)}\right)^{i}},

then the function

g\mapsto\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{1}d\xi\,\sqrt{\xi(1-\xi)}\,\varphi(\nu,g,\xi)

is differentiable in
g
and has a non singular development up to an error term
o(1-g)
.

The first singular terms in the development of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}^{\nu}\left[g^{|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{\infty}^{\nu}))|}\right]
then come from the integrals

I_{i}(\nu,g)=\frac{\phi_{1}(\nu,1,1)\,\aleph_{i}^{\beth^{Q^{+}}}(\nu)}{\beth^{\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}}(\nu)}\,\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{1}{d\xi}\,\sqrt{\xi(1-\xi)}\,\left(2-\frac{1}{1-\sqrt{g\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,z(\nu,g,\xi)}\right)^{-i},

for
i\in\left\{\frac{4}{3},\frac{2}{3},\frac{1}{3},0,-\frac{1}{3},-\frac{2}{3}\right\}
. We will only detail the case when
i=4/3
as the other cases are treated similarly and have singular terms of smaller order.

We can simplify
I_{4/3}
into

I_{4/3}(\nu,g)=\frac{\phi_{1}(\nu,1,1)\,\aleph_{4/3}^{\beth^{Q^{+}}}(\nu)}{\beth^{\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}}(\nu)}\,\left(\frac{1-2\sqrt{g\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,c_{-}^{\nu}(g)}{1-\sqrt{g\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,c_{-}^{\nu}(g)}\right)^{-4/3}\\
\times\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{1}{d\xi}\,\sqrt{\xi(1-\xi)}\,\left(\frac{1-\dfrac{2\sqrt{g\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,(c_{+}^{\nu}(g)-c_{-}^{\nu}(g))}{1-2\sqrt{g\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,c_{-}^{\nu}(g)}\xi}{1-\dfrac{\sqrt{g\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,(c_{+}^{\nu}(g)-c_{-}^{\nu}(g))}{1-\sqrt{g\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,c_{-}^{\nu}(g)}\xi}\right)^{-4/3}.

In this last quantity, we have as
g\to 1^{-}
:

\frac{\sqrt{g\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,(c_{+}^{\nu}(g)-c_{-}^{\nu}(g))}{1-\sqrt{g\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,c_{-}^{\nu}(g)}\to\frac{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{y_{-}^{\nu}}}{1-\frac{1}{y_{-}^{\nu}}}<1\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{2\sqrt{g\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,(c_{+}^{\nu}(g)-c_{-}^{\nu}(g))}{1-2\sqrt{g\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,c_{-}^{\nu}(g)}\to 1^{-}.

The same line of reasoning as before shows that the leading singular term will come from the numerator and will be given by the leading singular term of

\displaystyle I(\nu,g)

\displaystyle=\frac{\phi_{1}(\nu,1,1)\,\aleph_{4/3}^{\beth^{Q^{+}}}(\nu)}{\beth^{\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}}(\nu)}\,\left(\frac{1-2\sqrt{g\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,c_{-}^{\nu}}{1-\sqrt{g\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,c_{-}^{\nu}}\right)^{-4/3}\,\left(1-\frac{\sqrt{\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,(c_{+}^{\nu}-c_{-}^{\nu})}{1-\sqrt{\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,c_{-}^{\nu}}\right)^{4/3}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\frac{1}{\pi}\int_{0}^{1}{d\xi}\,\xi^{1/2}(1-\xi)^{1/2}\,\left(1-\frac{2\sqrt{g\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,(c_{+}^{\nu}(g)-c_{-}^{\nu}(g))}{1-2\sqrt{g\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,c_{-}^{\nu}(g)}\xi\right)^{-4/3},

\displaystyle=\frac{\phi_{1}(\nu,1,1)\,\aleph_{4/3}^{\beth^{Q^{+}}}(\nu)}{\beth^{\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}}(\nu)}\,\left(2-\frac{4}{y_{-}^{\nu}}\right)^{-4/3}\,_{2}F_{1}\left(\frac{4}{3},\frac{3}{2};3;\frac{2\sqrt{g\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,(c_{+}^{\nu}(g)-c_{-}^{\nu}(g))}{1-2\sqrt{g\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,c_{-}^{\nu}(g)}\right).

We also have

\frac{2\sqrt{g\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,(c_{+}^{\nu}(g)-c_{-}^{\nu}(g))}{1-2\sqrt{g\,\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,c_{-}^{\nu}(g)}\\
=1-\frac{2\sqrt{\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,\left({\sqrt{z^{+}}}\aleph^{\diamond}(\nu)+z^{+}\aleph^{\bullet}(\nu)\right)^{-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}}{1-2/y_{-}^{\nu}}\,\left(1-g\right)^{\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}+o\left(\left(1-g\right)^{\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}\right).

So that, by standard singular expansion of hypergeometric functions at
1
, the leading singular term of
I(\nu,g)
is

-\mathrm{C}(\nu)\,\left(1-g\right)^{\frac{1}{6(a(\nu)-1/2)}}

with

\mathrm{C}(\nu)=\frac{\phi_{1}(\nu,1,1)\,\aleph_{4/3}^{\beth^{Q^{+}}}(\nu)}{\beth^{\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}}(\nu)}\,\left(2-\frac{4}{y_{-}^{\nu}}\right)^{-4/3}\,\frac{54\cdot 2^{\frac{1}{3}}\cdot\Gamma(\frac{2}{3})^{3}}{\pi^{2}}\\
\cdot\left(\frac{2\sqrt{\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,\left({\sqrt{z^{+}}}\aleph^{\diamond}(\nu)+z^{+}\aleph^{\bullet}(\nu)\right)^{-\frac{1}{a(\nu)-1/2}}}{1-2/y_{-}^{\nu}}\right)^{1/6}.

This term is also the leading singular term of
\mathbb{E}_{\infty}^{\nu}\left[g^{|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{\infty}^{\nu}))|}\right]
and we finally get:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\infty}^{\nu}\left[g^{|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{\infty}^{\nu}))|}\right]

\displaystyle=1-\mathrm{C}(\nu)\,\left(1-g\right)^{\frac{1}{6(a(\nu)-1/2)}}+o\left(\left(1-g\right)^{\frac{1}{6(a(\nu)-1/2)}}\right).

As in the proof of Theorem
1.3
, this expansion is not quite enough to get the asymptotic behavior of
\mathbb{P}^{\nu}_{\infty}\left(|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{\infty}^{\nu}))|=n\right)
because of possible complex singularities of modulus
1
(which we do not believe to exist). It is however enough to get the tail probabilities:

\frac{1-\mathbb{E}_{\infty}^{\nu}\left[g^{|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{\infty}^{\nu}))|}\right]}{1-g}=\sum_{n\geqslant 0}\mathbb{P}^{\nu}_{\infty}\left(|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{\infty}^{\nu}))|>n\right)\,g^{n}\underset{g\to 1^{-}}{\sim}\mathrm{C}(\nu)\,\left(1-g\right)^{\frac{1}{6(a(\nu)-1/2)}-1},

and the classical Tauberian theorem (see e.g. Theorem VI.13 of
[
36
]
and the following discussion) gives

\mathbb{P}^{\nu}_{\infty}\left(|V(\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{\infty}^{\nu}))|>n\right)\underset{n\to\infty}{\sim}\frac{\mathrm{C}(\nu)}{\Gamma\left(1-\frac{1}{6(a(\nu)-1/2)}\right)}\,n^{-\frac{1}{6(a(\nu)-1/2)}},

finishing the proof of Theorem
1.2
.

7
Cluster interface and Looptrees

In this section, we study the geometry of the root spin cluster boundary seen as a discrete looptree. Following the work of Curien and Kortchemski in the setting of site percolation on the UIPT in
[
28
]
, we encode the boundary of the root spin cluster by a two type Galton–Watson tree. The asymptotic properties of this tree can be fully characterized thanks to the enumerative results on triangulations with simple monochromatic boundary obtained in section
3.5
. The arguments developed in
[
28
]
can then be carried out to our setting without major difficulties to prove Theorem
1.5
.

7.1
Encoding by a two type Galton–Watson tree

Recall the definition of discrete looptrees given in the introduction and the construction that associates to a tree
\tau
, the looptree
\mathrm{Loop}(\tau)
, as illustrated in Figure
3
.

We can define a different mapping that associates a tree
\mathrm{Tree}_{2}(L)
with alternating white and black vertices and rooted at a white vertex to a looptree
L
as follows: we put a black vertex inside each cycle of
L
(except the exterior cycle), and connect it to each white vertex of
L
that belongs to this cycle. Notice that the perimeter of
L
is equal to the total number of vertices of
\mathrm{Tree}_{2}(L)
. This mapping is bijective, we denote by
\mathrm{Loop}_{2}(\tau)
the looptree associated to an alternating white and black tree
\tau
rooted at a white vertex. The index
2
in these two mappings is to stress out the fact that the corresponding trees have two alternating colors as we have the similar construction
\mathrm{Loop}(\tau)
for uncolored trees. See Figure
9
for an illustration.

Figure 9:
A looptree
L
, its associated alternating two type tree
\tau=\mathrm{Tree}_{2}(L)
, the tree associated by Janson-Stefánsson bijection
\mathrm{JS}(\tau)
, and the looptree
\mathrm{Loop}(\mathrm{JS}(\tau))
. Contracting the red edges of
\mathrm{Loop}(\mathrm{JS}(\tau))
gives back
L
.

For
\nu>0
, define the two probability measures
\mu^{\circ}_{\nu}
and
\mu^{\bullet}_{\nu}
on
\mathbb{Z}^{+}
by setting, for
k\geqslant 0
:

\displaystyle\mu^{\circ}_{\nu}(k)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1+Z^{+}(\nu,t_{\nu},t_{\nu}x_{\nu})}\,\left(\frac{Z^{+}(\nu,t_{\nu},t_{\nu}x_{\nu})}{1+Z^{+}(\nu,t_{\nu},t_{\nu}x_{\nu})}\right)^{k},

\displaystyle\mu^{\bullet}_{\nu}(k)

\displaystyle=\frac{(t_{\nu}x_{\nu})^{k+1}\,Z_{k+1}^{+}(\nu,t_{\nu})}{Z^{+}(\nu,t_{\nu},t_{\nu}x_{\nu})}.

Denote by
\mathrm{GW}_{\mu^{\circ}_{\nu},\mu^{\bullet}_{\nu}}
the law of an alternating two type Galton-Watson tree with respective offspring distributions
\mu^{\circ}_{\nu}
for white vertices (having black offspring) and
\mu^{\bullet}_{\nu}
for black vertices (having white offspring).

Proposition 7.1
.

For every
\nu>0
and every alternating two type tree
\tau
rooted at a white vertex, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\nu}

\displaystyle(\mathrm{Tree}_{2}(\partial\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}_{\infty}))=\tau||\mathfrak{H}(T^{\nu}_{\infty})|<\infty)

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{\delta_{|V(\tau)|-1}(\nu)\,x_{\nu}^{1-|V(\tau)|}\,\left(1+Z^{+}(\nu,t_{\nu},t_{\nu}x_{\nu})\right)^{|V(\tau)|+1}}{Z^{+}(\nu,t_{\nu},t_{\nu}x_{\nu})}\mathrm{GW}_{\mu^{\circ}_{\nu},\mu^{\bullet}_{\nu}}(\tau).

In particular, the law of
\mathrm{Tree}_{2}(\partial\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}_{\infty}))
conditioned on
|\partial\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}_{\infty})|=n
and
|\mathfrak{H}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}_{\infty})|<\infty
is
\mathrm{GW}_{\mu^{\circ}_{\nu},\mu^{\bullet}_{\nu}}
conditioned to have
n
vertices.

Proof.

Fix
\mathfrak{h}
a finite triangulation with spins and a boundary (not necessarily simple) with positive spins. Using Proposition
4.14
we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{n}^{\nu}\left(\mathfrak{H}(\mathfrak{C})=\mathfrak{h}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{[t^{3n}]\left(\nu^{m(\mathfrak{h})}t^{|\mathfrak{h}|}\cdot(\nu t)^{-|\partial\mathfrak{h}|/2}q_{|\partial\mathfrak{h}|}(\nu,t)\right)}{[t^{3n}]\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}(\nu,t)},

\displaystyle\underset{n\to\infty}{\rightarrow}\nu^{m(\mathfrak{h})}t_{\nu}^{|\mathfrak{h}|}\cdot t_{\nu}^{|\partial\mathfrak{h}|}\delta_{|\partial\mathfrak{h}|}(\nu).

As a consequence, by continuity with respect to the local topology, we have:

\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\nu}(\mathfrak{H}(\mathfrak{C})=\mathfrak{h})=\nu^{m(\mathfrak{h})}t_{\nu}^{|\mathfrak{h}|}\cdot t_{\nu}^{|\partial\mathfrak{h}|}\delta_{|\partial\mathfrak{h}|}(\nu).

(7.1)

Fix
\tau
an alternating white and black tree rooted at a white vertex and denote by
|\partial\tau|
the perimeter of
\mathrm{Loop}_{2}(\tau)
. Summing (
7.1
) over every hull having the same looptree as boundary we get:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\nu}(\mathrm{Tree}(\partial\mathfrak{C})=\tau||\mathfrak{H}(\mathfrak{C})|<\infty)

\displaystyle=\delta_{|\partial\tau|}(\nu)\,t_{\nu}^{|\partial\tau|}\,\sum_{\mathfrak{h}\,:\,\partial\mathfrak{h}=\mathrm{Loop}(\tau)}\nu^{m(\mathfrak{h})}t_{\nu}^{|\mathfrak{h}|},

\displaystyle=\delta_{|\partial\tau|}(\nu)\,t_{\nu}^{|\partial\tau|}\,\prod_{v\in V_{\bullet}(\tau)}Z_{\mathrm{deg}(v)}^{+}(\nu,t_{\nu}),

\displaystyle=\delta_{|\partial\tau|}(\nu)\,\prod_{v\in V_{\bullet}(\tau)}t_{\nu}^{\mathrm{deg}(v)}\,Z_{\mathrm{deg}(v)}^{+}(\nu,t_{\nu}),

\displaystyle=\delta_{|\partial\tau|}(\nu)\,x_{\nu}^{-|\partial\tau|}\,\prod_{v\in V_{\bullet}(\tau)}(t_{\nu}x_{\nu})^{\mathrm{deg}(v)}\,Z_{\mathrm{deg}(v)}^{+}(\nu,t_{\nu}).

By construction of
\mathrm{Loop}_{2}(\tau)
, we have
\sum_{u\in V_{\bullet}(\tau)}\mathrm{deg}(u)=|\partial\tau|
. Moreover, since
\mathrm{Loop}_{2}(\tau)
is an alternating two-type tree, we clearly have:

\sum_{u\in V_{\circ}(\tau)}\mathrm{deg}(u)=|E(\tau)|=|V(\tau)|+1=|V_{\circ}(\tau)|+|V_{\bullet}(\tau)|+1.

Hence, we get for any
a>0

\displaystyle\frac{1}{a}

\displaystyle=\left(\prod_{u\in V_{\circ}(\tau)}\frac{1}{a^{\mathrm{deg}(u)-1}}\right)\,\left(\prod_{v\in V_{\bullet}(\tau)}a\right),

\displaystyle=\left(1-\frac{1}{a}\right)^{-|V(\tau)|}\,\left(\prod_{u\in V_{\circ}(\tau)}\left(1-\frac{1}{a}\right)\frac{1}{a^{\mathrm{deg}(u)-1}}\right)\,\left(\prod_{v\in V_{\bullet}(\tau)}(a-1)\right).

Setting

a=1+\frac{1}{Z^{+}(\nu,t_{\nu},t_{\nu}x_{\nu})},

we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\infty}^{\nu}

\displaystyle(\mathrm{Tree}_{2}(\partial\mathfrak{C})=\tau||\mathfrak{H}(\mathfrak{C})|<\infty)

\displaystyle\qquad=\delta_{|V(\tau)|-1}(\nu)\,x_{\nu}^{1-|V(\tau)|}\,\left(1+Z^{+}(\nu,t_{\nu},t_{\nu}x_{\nu})\right)^{|V(\tau)|}\,\frac{1+Z^{+}(\nu,t_{\nu},t_{\nu}x_{\nu})}{Z^{+}(\nu,t_{\nu},t_{\nu}x_{\nu})}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\left(\prod_{u\in V_{\circ}(\tau)}\frac{1}{1+Z^{+}(\nu,t_{\nu},t_{\nu}x_{\nu})}\left(\frac{Z^{+}(\nu,t_{\nu},t_{\nu}x_{\nu})}{1+Z^{+}(\nu,t_{\nu},t_{\nu}x_{\nu})}\right)^{\mathrm{deg}(u)-1}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\times\left(\prod_{v\in V_{\bullet}(\tau)}\frac{(t_{\nu}x_{\nu})^{\mathrm{deg}(v)}\,Z_{\mathrm{deg}(v)}^{+}(\nu,t_{\nu})}{Z^{+}(\nu,t_{\nu},t_{\nu}x_{\nu})}\right),

which finishes the proof, since the tree is rooted at a white vertex.
∎

The case of finite random triangulations is very similar:

Proposition 7.2
.

For every
\nu>0
and every alternating two type tree
\tau
rooted at a white vertex, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\nu}

\displaystyle(\mathrm{Tree}_{2}(\partial\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}))=\tau)

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{q_{|V(\tau)|-1}(\nu,t_{\nu})\,(\sqrt{\nu t_{\nu}^{3}}\,x_{\nu})^{1-|V(\tau)|}\,\left(1+Z^{+}(\nu,t_{\nu},t_{\nu}x_{\nu})\right)^{|V(\tau)|+1}}{\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}(\nu,t_{\nu})\,Z^{+}(\nu,t_{\nu},t_{\nu}x_{\nu})}\mathrm{GW}_{\mu^{\circ}_{\nu},\mu^{\bullet}_{\nu}}(\tau).

In particular, the law of
\mathrm{Tree}_{2}(\partial\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu}))
conditioned on
|\partial\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}^{\nu})|=n
is
\mathrm{GW}_{\mu^{\circ}_{\nu},\mu^{\bullet}_{\nu}}
conditioned to have
n
vertices.

Proof.

By definition we have

\mathbb{P}^{\nu}(\mathfrak{H}(\mathfrak{C})=\mathfrak{h})=\frac{1}{\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}(\nu,t_{\nu})}\nu^{m(\mathfrak{h})}t_{\nu}^{|\mathfrak{h}|}\cdot(\nu\,t_{\nu})^{-|\partial\mathfrak{h}|/2}q_{|\partial\mathfrak{h}|}(\nu).

From there, the same calculation as in the proof of Proposition
7.1
gives

\mathbb{P}^{\nu}(\mathrm{Tree}(\partial\mathfrak{C})=\tau)=\frac{q_{|\partial\tau|}(\nu)\,(\nu t_{\nu}^{3})^{-|\partial\tau|/2}\,x_{\nu}^{-|\partial\tau|}}{\mathcal{Z}^{\oplus}(\nu,t_{\nu})}\,\,\prod_{v\in V_{\bullet}(\tau)}(t_{\nu}x_{\nu})^{\mathrm{deg}(v)}\,Z_{\mathrm{deg}(v)}^{+}(\nu,t_{\nu}),

and the result follows as in the proof of Proposition
7.1
.
∎

7.2
Proof of Theorem
1.5

We are now ready to prove Theorem
1.5
. The same proof can originally be found in
[
28
, Proof of Theorem 1.2]
in the setting of site percolation on the UIPT. We adapt it here in our setting for the sake of completeness.

Proof of Theorem
1.5
.

The proofs for
\partial\mathfrak{C}_{\infty}^{\nu}(n)
and for
\partial\mathfrak{C}^{\nu}(n)
are identical since both of them rely on the law of the associated two type Galton–Watson trees, which is the same by Propositions
7.1
and
7.2
. We only write the details in the infinite volume case.

Recall from the introduction that
\partial\mathfrak{C}_{\infty}^{\nu}(n)
denotes
\partial\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{\infty}^{\nu})
conditioned on the event that
\mathfrak{H}(\mathbf{T}_{\infty}^{\nu})
is finite and that the perimeter of
\mathfrak{C}(\mathbf{T}_{\infty}^{\nu})
is
n
. Proposition
7.1
ensures that
\mathrm{Tree}_{2}(\partial\mathfrak{C}_{\infty}^{\nu}(n))
has law
\mathrm{GW}_{\mu^{\circ}_{\nu},\mu^{\bullet}_{\nu}}^{(n)}
, which is the law
\mathrm{GW}_{\mu^{\circ}_{\nu},\mu^{\bullet}_{\nu}}
conditioned on the event that the tree has
n
vertices.

We can use the Janson–Stefánsson bijection
[
42
]
to map alternating two type trees rooted at a white vertex and uncolored rooted trees. For an alternating two type tree
\tau
, we denote by
\mathrm{JS}(\tau)
its image by the Janson–Stefánsson bijection. It is not needed to know details about this bijection as all the arguments used to prove Theorem
1.5
are established in
[
28
]
. The illustration given in Figure
9
should suffice for our purpose.

It is proved in
[
42
, Appendix A]
and in
[
28
, Proposition 3.6]
) that the Janson–Stefánsson bijection
maps a two-type Galton–Watson tree with law
\mathrm{GW}_{\mu^{\circ}_{\nu},\mu^{\bullet}_{\nu}}
conditioned to have
n
vertices to a one type Galton–Watson tree conditioned to have
n
vertices and with reproduction law
\mu_{\nu}
defined by

\mu_{\nu}(k)=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{1+Z^{+}(\nu,t_{\nu},t_{\nu}x_{\nu})}&\text{for $k=0$},\\
\frac{Z^{+}(\nu,t_{\nu},t_{\nu}x_{\nu})}{1+Z^{+}(\nu,t_{\nu},t_{\nu}x_{\nu})}\,\mu^{\bullet}_{\nu}(k-1)&\text{for $k\geqslant 1$}.\end{cases}

Therefore,
\mathrm{JS}(\mathrm{Tree}_{2}(\partial\mathfrak{C}_{\infty}^{\nu}(n)))
is a Galton–Watson tree with reproduction law
\mu_{\nu}
conditioned to have
n
vertices.

The generating function of
\mu_{\nu}
is given by

G_{\mu_{\nu}}(z)=\sum_{k\geqslant 0}\mu_{\nu}(k)\,z^{k}=\frac{1+Z^{+}(\nu,t_{\nu},t_{\nu}x_{\nu}\,z)}{1+Z^{+}(\nu,t_{\nu},t_{\nu}x_{\nu})}.

According to Lemma
3.16
, this function is differentiable at
z=1
and we have

G_{\mu_{\nu}}^{\prime}(1)=\frac{-\aleph_{1}^{Z^{+}}(\nu)}{1+Z^{+}(\nu,t_{\nu},t_{\nu}x_{\nu})},

(7.2)

Hence, the distribution is critical when
\nu<\nu_{c}
and subcritical when
\nu\geqslant\nu_{c}
.
We can separate into two cases depending on whether the reproduction law
\mu_{\nu}
is critical or subcritical.

Case
\nu\geqslant\nu_{c}
.

In this case, the reproduction law
\mu_{\nu}
is subcritical. It is proved in
[
43
, Theorem 5.5]
that, with probability tending to
1
as
n\to\infty
, the random tree
\mathrm{JS}(\mathrm{Tree}_{2}(\partial\mathfrak{C}_{\infty}^{\nu}(n)))
has a unique vertex
v_{n}^{\star}
of maximal degree and that, in probability,

\frac{\mathrm{deg}(v_{n}^{\star})}{n}\underset{n\to\infty}{\rightarrow}1-G_{\mu_{\nu}}^{\prime}(1).

By
[
48
, Corollary 2]
, the maximal size of the connected components of
\mathrm{JS}(\mathrm{Tree}_{2}(\partial\mathfrak{C}_{\infty}^{\nu}(n)))\setminus\{v_{n}^{\star}\}
divided by
n
vanishes in probability as
n\to\infty
. Since
\mu_{\nu}^{\circ}
has exponential tails, the vertex
v_{n}^{\star}
corresponds necessarily to a black vertex of
\mathrm{Tree}_{2}(\partial\mathfrak{C}_{\infty}^{\nu}(n))
, and therefore to a macroscopic loop of
\partial\mathfrak{C}_{\infty}^{\nu}(n)
. In addition, the other loops of
